“Yes,” Mr. Oliphant said as she came to a helpless stop;—“I think under the circumstances Harlan might—might properly see fit to—” But here he, too, hesitated and seemed unable to continue.

Their son, however, understood them perfectly, and turned sharply to face them. “Of course I knew you’d ask it,” he said, and an old bitterness, long held down within him, came to the surface. “I knew you wanted me to let Dan have even that twenty-five thousand dollars grandfather left me. You really wanted me to let him throw it away along with his own, though you never spoke out and asked me to do it. Martha Shelby did, though. *She* spoke out plainly enough! The fact that grandfather gave it to me never entered her head. She only thought I was miserly for not putting it into Dan’s hands to be squandered. That’s what she thought, and I’ve understood all along that my mother and my father had a great deal the same feeling.”

“No, no,” his mother protested, for the bitterness in his voice had increased as he spoke. “We never reproached you, dear.”

===Chinese===

“是，”奥利芬特先生一见她一筹莫展地住了口，便接过话头；——“我想，在这样的情形下，哈兰或许——或许得体地愿意——”可到这里，他也迟疑起来，似乎接不下去了。

可是他们的儿子把他们俩的心思摸得一清二楚，猛地转过身来正对着他们。“不用说，我早知道你们要开这个口，”他说，一腔积压多年的旧苦水，这会儿涌上了表面。“我知道你们一直想叫我连外公留给我的那两万五千美元都让给丹。你们是真想叫我由着他，连他自己那份一道扔个精光，尽管你们从来没有明说，开口求我。玛莎·谢尔比可是明说过。*她*说得再直白没有了！外公把钱给了我，这个事实压根儿就没进过她的脑子。她只嫌我吝啬，不肯把钱交到丹手里去挥霍。她就是这么想的，而我一直明白，我的父亲母亲的心情跟这差不多是一路的。”

“不，不，”母亲分辩道，因为他说着说着，声音里的苦味越来越重。“我们从来没有责怪过你，亲爱的。”

===Original===

“No, not in words maybe.”

“No, not in any way,” she said. “It was right of you to take care of it, and you’d be right now to take care of what you’ll have. Your father and I only mean that now you have so *much*—”

“Now that I have so *much*,” Harlan echoed, “I ought to throw away part of it, even though grandma’s trusted me to save it from just this very wastage and to take care of every bit of it?”

===Chinese===

“是没有，也许没有用言语责怪过。”

“不，什么方式都没有过，”她说。“你当年看好那份钱，是对的；如今你把将到手的这份看好，也是对的。你爸爸和我只是想说，如今你有那么*多*了——”

“如今我有那么*多*了，”哈兰应声重复，“我就该把其中一部分扔掉，尽管外婆正是信得过我，才把钱从这种糟蹋里保下来、要我一分一毫都看好？”

===Original===

“No, no; it isn’t that,” Mrs. Oliphant said; and with pathetically naive artfulness she changed the basis of her appeal. “But you know, dear, you were just telling us how much Martha had wanted you to help Dan—she’s always been such a devoted friend of his—and you said that after she hears about mother’s bequest to you, she may take it as a kind of supplanting your brother, and it would be harder than ever for you to make her fond of you; so don’t you see—don’t you see what a splendid effect it would have on her now, when you’ve got so much, dear, and could spare it—don’t you see, if you’d—if you’d—”

“Yes, I see,” Harlan said grimly. “You think Martha might even admire me enough to marry me, if I’d say to Dan: ‘Here! I won’t accept all this that should have been yours. Here’s half of it.’ ”

“Oh, no,” she cried, “I didn’t mean half of it; I only meant you might—”

===Chinese===

“不，不；不是这个意思，”奥利芬特太太说；她使出一种令人心酸的稚拙伎俩，换了由头再来恳求。“可是你知道，亲爱的，你方才才跟我们说过，玛莎多么盼着你帮丹一把——她始终是丹那么忠心的朋友——你又说，等她听说妈把遗产留给了你，她也许会认为这等于顶替了你哥哥，你要讨她喜欢就比什么时候都难了；那么你还不明白吗——你还不明白，眼下你手头这么宽裕，亲爱的，又匀得出这一份来，这对她该有多好的影响——你还不明白，只要你肯——只要你肯——”

“明白了，我明白，”哈兰冷冷地说。“你们是觉得，只要我对丹说一句：‘喏！这一切本该归你的家当，我不肯收。这一半你拿去。’玛莎没准儿就会钦佩我钦佩到肯嫁我的地步。”

“哦，不，”她喊道，“我没有说一半的意思；我只是说，你或许——”

===Original===

“No,” Harlan said; “not *any*, mother—not a dime! I won’t impress Martha with a pose. I don’t want her or anybody else to like me because of a pose.”

“Would it be a pose,” Mr. Oliphant asked gravely, “to help your brother?”

“Wouldn’t it?” Harlan returned as gravely. “Isn’t it a pose to do something that isn’t natural to you, simply to make a woman admire you? I’d call that a pose, myself, though you may have another definition of the word. I’m not caring to get admiration that way, sir.”

“All right,” his father said, nodding, as the fragile edifice of Mrs. Oliphant’s gentle cunning was thus dispersed upon the air. “I should say you had the right spirit there. But why need it be an attitude? Wouldn’t you really *like* to help Dan out a little, Harlan?”

===Chinese===

“不，”哈兰说；“一*分*都不给，妈——一毛钱都不给！我不靠摆姿态去打动玛莎。我不愿意她或者随便什么人，因为一个姿态而看上我。”

“帮你哥哥一把，”奥利芬特先生郑重地问，“也算摆姿态吗？”

“不算吗？”哈兰同样郑重地回敬。“做一件天性里没有的事，单为了叫一个女人钦佩你，这不算姿态？要我说这就算姿态，尽管二位对这个词儿可以有别的定义。那样得来的钦佩，我不稀罕，先生。”

“好吧，”父亲点头道，奥利芬特太太那座温柔巧计搭成的脆弱楼阁，就这样消散在空气里。“要我说，你这个精神倒是对路的。可这为什么非得是一种姿态呢？哈兰，你心底里难道就*不*愿意稍稍帮丹一把吗？”

===Original===

Harlan sighed. “Not in a failure, sir. First and last he’s had a pretty long chance to prove what he could do with his Addition, and he’s no nearer succeeding today than he was when he began. Instead, he’s lost all his money and all his time. All he’s done was to spoil a farm.”

“But if he had some really substantial assistance, it’s not absolutely impossible he might—”

“No, sir,” Harlan said definitely; “I don’t believe in it, and I’ll never do it. I didn’t want to supplant him. I didn’t ask for what grandma’s done for me; I never did one thing to get it, or for the purpose of making her like me; and, as a matter of fact, she didn’t do it because she liked me. But she did know I’d take care of it, and I’m going to prove she was right about that, anyhow. I won’t throw any of it away on an attitude to make Martha Shelby think well of me. Of course she’ll be all the surer she’s right about me, now that I don’t do anything for him, though I have so *much*!” He picked up the copy of Mrs. Savage’s will from the table where his father had left it, and, sitting down again, prepared to look over it; but, as he placed in position the eyeglasses already necessary to him when he read, he sent a sidelong glance toward his parents, a glance in which there was the bitterness of an ancient pain. “I wouldn’t even throw any of it away to make my father and mother like me a little better, either,” he said.

Mrs. Oliphant cried out reproachfully: “Oh, Harlan!” and she would have said more; but her husband shook his head at her, and she was silent. Harlan finished his reading, set the manuscript down upon the table, and went away without speaking again, so that his parents were left to themselves and a thoughtful, somewhat melancholy silence.

===Chinese===

哈兰叹了口气。“不救注定要失败的事，先生。从头到尾，他证明自己拿新区能干出什么名堂的机会，已经够长的了；到今天，他离成功并不比动工那天更近一步。相反，他把钱赔光了，把光阴也赔光了。他干成的事，不过是糟蹋了一个农场。”

“可要是有人帮他一把、帮到实实处，他也不是全然没有可能——”

“不，先生，”哈兰一口回绝；“这件事我不信，也永远不会做。我没有想顶替他。外婆为我做的这一切，不是我要来的；为了得到它，为了讨她的喜欢，我不曾出过一分力；况且说实话，她这样做也并非因为喜欢我。可她的确知道我会看好这份钱，至少在这一点上，我要证明她没有看错。为了叫玛莎·谢尔比对我存一分好评，我不会从这笔钱里掏出一个子儿去做姿态。自然喽，如今我这么*有钱*，却什么也不为他做，她只会越发相信她看我是看对了！”他从桌上拿起父亲搁下的那份萨维奇老太太的遗嘱抄本，重新坐下来，预备通读一遍；可是，就在他把那副看书早已离不得的眼镜架端正的时候，他朝父母那边斜睇了一眼，那一瞥里藏着一种旧创的痛楚。“就连为了叫我的父亲母亲多喜欢我一点，我也不会从里面掏一个子儿，”他说。

奥利芬特太太嗔怪地喊出声来：“哦，哈兰！”她本还想再说几句，她丈夫却朝她摇了摇头，她便不作声了。哈兰把遗嘱读完，把文件放回桌上，一句再多的话也没有，径自去了，撇下父母两个，守着一片沉思的、颇为凄清的静默。

===Original===

Mrs. Oliphant broke it diffidently. “You don’t think mother ever dreamed that—”

“That he might help Dan? No; not with the Addition. Harlan’s right when he says that’s just what she trusted him not to do.”

“I didn’t mean that,” Mrs. Oliphant explained. “I mean—you know what he said about mother’s hoping—I mean his saying he thought mother had those wild ideas about Lena’s going away and—and Martha Shelby—”

“No,” her husband said. “No; I don’t think so. It seems unlikely. I don’t think your mother would have—”

“No,” Mrs. Oliphant assented thoughtfully. “I can’t believe she would. Of course there isn’t any way of being sure—now.”

===Chinese===

奥利芬特太太怯怯地打破了静默。“你想妈会不会连做梦也没想到——”

“——想到他会去帮丹？不会；只要事关新区，就绝不会。哈兰说得对，外婆正是信着他不会去干这件事。”

“我不是说这个，”奥利芬特太太解释。“我是说——他讲的妈的那番指望，你是听见了的——我是说，他说他以为妈有过那些荒唐念头，什么莉娜会走，还有——还有玛莎·谢尔比——”

“是啊，”奥利芬特太太若有所思地应道。“我不信她会。当然，如今也再没有法子证实了——如今。”

“是没有法子；不过这多半只是哈兰的想象罢了。他敏感，而敏感向来也就意味着富于想象。我看咱们不必揪着这个不放。”

===Original===

“No; but it’s probably just Harlan’s imagination. He’s sensitive, and that always means imaginative, too. I don’t think we need to dwell on it.”

“I suppose not. Especially as she *couldn’t* have meant anything like that. You don’t think she *could*, do you, dear?”

“No, no; I don’t think so,” he answered. “We’d better be worrying over other matters, I suspect.”

“You mean about getting Harlan to help Dan out?”

“Yes.”

“Of course I can do something,” she said. “I’ll help all I can with the income mother’s given me; we’ve always managed to live very comfortably without it. But Harlan—why, I almost believe Dan could make a success of the Addition, if Harlan would do something substantial about it. Yes; we ought to be able to think of *some* way to get him to do it.”

===Chinese===

“是没有法子；不过这多半只是哈兰的想象罢了。他敏感，而敏感向来也就意味着富于想象。我看咱们不必揪着这个不放。”

“我看也是不必。何况她*断然*不会有那样的意思。你总不至于认为她*会*吧，亲爱的？”

“不，不；我不这样想，”他答道。“依我看，咱们还是去操心别的要紧事吧。”

“你是说，劝哈兰拉丹一把的事？”

“正是。”

“我这边自然有法子可想，”她说。“妈给我的那笔收益，我要倾力帮他；没了它，我们也向来过得很宽裕。可是哈兰——嗐，我几乎相信，只要哈兰肯为新区出一点实实在在的力，丹是能成功的。对；我们总该想得出*什么*法子，叫他肯这么办。”

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===

They thought of many ways to get him to do it, but none of such ingenuity as to inspire them with confidence. Mrs. Oliphant made more suggestions than her husband did, and she put most of them into the form of little dramatic dialogues imagined as taking place between Mr. Oliphant and Harlan. Mr. Oliphant was to say such-and-such things to Harlan, who would necessarily reply in certain terms, which she sketched;—whereupon his father could triumphantly turn the words just uttered into proof that Harlan would not only be doing his duty by helping Dan, but at the same time would make great headway with Martha Shelby in a straightforward manner involving not the slightest pose.

Unfortunately, after each of these small dramas in turn, becoming eager in her opinion that “*this* time” she had “got it,” she was forced into pessimism by Mr. Oliphant’s pointing out that Harlan wouldn’t say what she had sketched for him; but, on the contrary, was certain to express himself to an effect precisely the opposite.

Many times that afternoon the poor lady murmured, “No, I suppose perhaps it wouldn’t do after all,” and pondered again. “But why don’t you think of a way that *would* do?” she asked, with more spirit, after one of her failures. “You’re a lawyer; you ought to be able to think of *something*.”

He laughed and made the gesture of a man helpless between opposing viewpoints of his own. “What provokes me is that I can’t help seeing Harlan’s side of it, too. There’s a good deal to be said on his side, you know.”

===Chinese===

为了让他肯办这件事，他们想了许多法子，却没有一个巧到足以叫他们自己有了信心。出主意出得比她丈夫多的，是奥利芬特太太（Mrs. Oliphant）；而且她把大部分主意都编成了小小的戏剧性对话，设想由奥利芬特先生（Mr. Oliphant）和哈兰（Harlan）来演。奥利芬特先生只须如此这般地对哈兰说上几句，哈兰便必然会照她拟好的词句作答；——于是做父亲的便可乘胜进逼，把儿子刚一出口的话当场变成论据，证明哈兰帮丹（Dan）不但是尽了他应尽的本分，而且还能借这个光明正大、不带半点做作的法子，在玛莎·谢尔比（Martha Shelby）跟前大进一步。

可惜，每一出这样的小戏轮番演过，她都越发热切，认定“*这*一回”她总算是“成了”，却总被奥利芬特先生一句话打回悲观里去：哈兰才不会照她拟的那样说呢；恰恰相反，他一开口，说出的准是意思正好相反的话。

那天下午，这位可怜的太太一遍又一遍地咕哝：“不成，我想恐怕终究还是不成，”然后又重新捉摸。“可你干吗不想一个*行得通*的法子呢？”一回失败过后，她提着精神问道。“你是律师，总该想得出*点什么*来。”

他笑了，摊开两手，活像一个人叫自己心里两个对着干的主意夹在中间、无可如何。“叫我恼火的是，哈兰那一面的道理我也没法不看进眼里。你知道，替他说话的理由多着呢。”

===Original===

“Yes, indeed,” she readily assented. “He thinks he’s perfectly right; but of course he isn’t.”

“Well, why isn’t he? After all, your mother trusted him to do just what we’re planning to get him not to do.”

“But her will doesn’t *say* he can’t help Dan. So why shouldn’t he?”

“No,” Mr. Oliphant interrupted; “it doesn’t say he mustn’t; but that’s what she counted on. In our hearts we’re blaming him for not betraying a trust, and for being unwilling to put money into the fire;—he honestly believes it would be putting it into the fire. And he won’t do it, even though he knows his refusing makes him look mean in the eyes of pretty much everybody he cares about, even in the eyes of the person he seems to care most about. Well, there’s something rather fine in a stand like that, after all.”

===Chinese===

“是啊，可不是，”她一口附和。“他自以为百分之百有理；可他当然没有理。”

“呃，他怎么就没有理？说到底，你母亲正是信托他去办那件事，而我们如今的盘算，恰恰是要劝得他不办。”

“可她的遗嘱又没*写着*不许他帮丹。那他为什么不该帮？”

“话不是这么说，”奥利芬特先生打断她；“遗嘱是没写他不可以；可老太太指靠的正是这个。咱们心里责怪他，怪的是他不肯辜负所托，怪的是他不肯把钱往火里扔；——他可是真心相信那等于把钱往火里扔。他不肯干，尽管他也明白，这一拒绝会叫他在几乎所有他在乎的人眼里显得小气，甚至在他看上去最在乎的那一位眼里也是一样。唉，说到底，这样的立场里头，毕竟有几分相当可贵的东西。”

===Original===

“Martha’d never forgive him!” Mrs. Oliphant said emphatically. “Never! If he doesn’t help Dan, now that he’s got so *much*, she’d always believe him terribly stingy. So you see we ought to persuade him for his own good, too—if we could only think of a way.”

But they continued to find that elusive way beset by baffling afterthoughts; and when Dan came home from his excursion, successful and in high fettle, they spoke to him of the subject that had been engrossing them—and were straightway baffled again. Dan even declined the proffer of future assistance from his mother.

“Not a penny!” he said. “She didn’t have any faith in me, and she despised the whole idea of Ornaby. She gave me thirty-five hundred dollars of my own—bless her for it! She gave me that to do with as I please, and it’s plenty. Why, tomorrow I’m goin’ to fix up the interest on what’s owed on the land, and then I’ve got to settle another little matter, and after that I—”

“Wait, Dan,” his father interposed. “What other little matter is it you have to settle? I didn’t know anything had been worrying you except the probable foreclosure.”

===Chinese===

“玛莎绝不会饶他！”奥利芬特太太着重地说。“绝不会！如今他手头有了*那么多*钱，他若不帮丹，玛莎往后会一直认定他小气得吓人。所以你瞧，就算单为他自己好，我们也该劝他——只要我们能想出那条法子来。”

可他们后来发觉，那条飘忽难寻的路，仍旧四面叫一层层想不完的难题围着；等丹出门归来，大功告成、兴致极高，他们向他提起那件占满了他们心头的事——登时又碰了壁。连他母亲主动提出的日后接济，丹也谢绝了。

“一个子儿也别想！”他说。“她信不过我，奥纳比这整个主意她都瞧不上眼。她给了我三千五百块，是我自己的——单为这个得谢她！这笔钱她给了我，随我爱怎么使就怎么使，这就尽够了。喏，明儿我就去把买地欠款的利息料理清楚，跟着还有一桩小事情要办，办完那桩我就——”

“慢着，丹，”他父亲插话道。“你还要了结的是一桩什么小事情？我只晓得那桩说不定的取消赎回权叫你发愁，别的事我可没听说啊。”

===Original===

“It didn’t, sir. I didn’t worry about this at all. I knew I could fix *it* all right, if I could just hold off the foreclosure. It seems I’ve never paid any of the taxes on the Addition—I’ve had so many other things on my mind, it seems I just kind of neglected that—and so somebody’s got a tax title to it; but now I can settle with him tomorrow morning and clear it off—and then I’m goin’ to turn up some sod out there! I’m goin’ to get ready to lay the foundation for my first factory!”

“But the *money*, dear!” his mother cried. “How in the world do you expect even to lay the foundations unless we can get Harlan—”

“No, ma’am! I wouldn’t take a nickel of it if he begged me to! I’ve been pretty near where I was ready to steal to get money to pull me out of a hole; but I’ll never take one single cent of what grandma left Harlan, or of what she left you either. If she’d meant me to have it she’d have given it to me herself; but she didn’t have any faith in me, and she says so in plain words in her will. You don’t expect me to take help from her that she wanted to *prevent*, do you? Never in this world!”

“There! You see?” Mrs. Oliphant lamented, appealing to her husband. “I knew it hurt him, in spite of what he said. I knew it!”

===Chinese===

“没愁过，先生。这个我压根儿没发过愁。我知道只要把取消赎回权的事拖住，*它*准能摆平。说起来，新区的税我好像一直一块儿也没缴过——我心里搁的事太多了，好像就这么把这一茬疏忽了——结果叫人攥下了它的税契；不过明儿一早我就能跟他了清这笔账，把这事抹平——跟着我就要上那儿翻开几块生草皮！我要预备给我头一座工厂打地基啦！”

“可是*钱*呢，亲爱的！”他母亲嚷道。“不把哈兰说动，你究竟打算连地基都怎么打呀——”

“不，太太！他就是求我，我也一个镍板儿也不拿！为着弄钱拉我出坑，我差点儿就到了要去偷的地步；可外婆留给哈兰的钱，或是留给你的钱，我一个铜板也决计不碰。她要是真想给我，早就亲手给我了；她信不过我，遗嘱里白纸黑字就是这么说的。她成心*拦着*不给的帮衬，你总不至于想叫我伸手去接吧？死也不能！”

“听听！你听见了吧？”奥利芬特太太朝她丈夫诉苦道。“我说他心里疼着呢，嘴上不认。我就知道！”

===Original===

“You’re all wrong,” Dan stoutly maintained. “She kind of explained to me what she was goin’ to do, though I didn’t see what she meant. It was just a few minutes before she died. She told me to remember not to be hurt, but she needn’t have worried about it, and I told her so. So don’t *you* worry about it. I didn’t begin to build Ornaby on my expectations from her; I’ve carried it along this far by myself, and I expect to carry it the rest of the way. And I’m goin’ to build that factory! George McMillan thinks maybe he can float some of the stock for it in New York, and I don’t know but he’s got a little money of his own he may want to put in. The way I feel, why, it looks to me as if I was about ready to climb out on the top o’ the heap right now; and I’m certainly not baby enough to be hurt because my grandmother didn’t have any faith in me.”

He continued to protest and perhaps protested too much; for although it was clear enough to his parents that he was so heartened by his thirty-five hundred dollars as to anticipate miracles, yet it was not to be believed that his pride had suffered no injury at all. What appeared in his grandmother’s will as a severe criticism of his ability and judgment was more than a mere neutral lack of faith; and Mrs. Oliphant’s intuition had touched the truth; he was indeed hurt—but he never admitted it.

Moreover, he remained steadfast in refusal; he would neither allow his mother to help him with money nor countenance any appeal of hers, or his father’s, to Harlan. Both of them, uncountenanced, did with faint hope reopen the subject to Harlan, though they did it indirectly;—they made allusions to the pathos of the brave and independent position his brother had taken. But Harlan only looked slightly badgered, and replied that this extolled position of Dan’s was the only possible correct one under the circumstances.

From time to time the troubled parents tried other diplomacies of increasing feebleness, until finally it seemed best to mention the subject, even indirectly, no more. In the evenings the silences in the library were charged with feeling withheld from expression; though Dan enlivened the room when he came in, and made it boisterous if he brought the baby with him. Certainly no depression could be recorded against either of this pair; Henry Daniel glowed with health and became livelier with every month of increasing age and weight. As for Lena, after her outburst upon the reading of Mrs. Savage’s will, she was another of this household who was surcharged with repressed feeling; but her repression became a habit;—weeks went by when she did not slam a door. She appeared to become more tolerant of her husband at this period than she had ever been; and when she spoke to him at all, it was in a tone suggesting that her tolerance had in it something of compassion.

===Chinese===

“你们全想岔了，”丹一口咬定。“她那个安排，多少跟我交代过一句，虽然我当时没听懂她的意思。就在她去世前几分钟。她叮嘱我记着别往心里去；其实这层她原用不着担心，我当时就这么对她说了。所以*你们*也别为这个担心。我当初起奥纳比的头，靠的不是指望她那份遗产；一路干到今天，是我自己扛下来的，往后的路我也打算自己扛到底。还有，那座工厂我是造定了！乔治·麦克米伦（George McMillan）说，它的股票没准儿能由他在纽约张罗出一部分，而且说不定他自己还有几个闲钱，也想投进来呢。照我眼下这股劲儿，嘿，我看我简直是马上就要爬到那一堆东西的顶儿上去了；外婆信不过我，我总不至于娇气得连这个都要受伤。”

他这样表白下去，或许表白得过了头；因为虽说父母都看得明白：那三千五百块叫他精神大振，简直指望起奇迹来，可要说他的自尊心没受半点挫伤，谁也不会信。外祖母的遗嘱里那份对他的才干和判断力的严厉评断，绝不止是一种不偏不倚的不信任；奥利芬特太太的直觉触到了真相：他确实受了伤——只是他从不认账。

不但如此，他一口回绝，绝不动摇；他既不许母亲拿钱帮他，也不容她或是父亲去向哈兰开口。夫妻二人得不到他的准话，还是抱着一线微茫的希望，向哈兰重提过这事，虽然提得很绕弯子；——他们感慨了一番他兄弟所取的那个勇敢而独立的立场是如何动人。哈兰却只略微露出一点被缠磨得无奈的神气，答道：丹这个如此可嘉的立场，正是在眼下情势中唯一可能的正确立场。

忧心的父母时不时还试过别的斡旋之法，却一路疲软下去；到后来，索性觉得这事连拐弯抹角也不再提为妙。晚间，藏书室里的沉默满蓄着压住不发的情绪；不过丹一进屋，屋子便有了活气，他若再带上娃娃来，满屋更要闹腾成一团。这一对父子，谁身上也记不下半点消沉：亨利·丹尼尔（Henry Daniel）健旺得发亮，月月见长，月月更活泼。至于莉娜（Lena），自宣读萨维奇老太太（Mrs. Savage）遗嘱那天她发作过一场之后，她也成了这个家里另一个郁结满腔的人；只是她的郁结渐渐成了习惯；——一连几个星期，她连一扇门都不摔了。这个时期，她对丈夫显得比以往任何时候都更容让；偶尔同他说话，那口气也听得出，她的容让里掺着几分怜惜。

===Original===

She devoted herself to her baby, perhaps finding a refuge in her devotion; but she declined to accompany Dan on Sunday afternoons when he went for a sidewalk excursion with the perambulator. This was an established custom in the town, she observed: every Sunday, early in the afternoon, the young fathers and mothers began to appear upon the sidewalks, the fathers pushing the baby-carriages and the mothers strolling a little way behind with the toddlers, if there were any of these, or perhaps lingering for a moment of gossip with friends encountered by the way, then scurrying on to overtake the perambulator.

High and low followed the custom; it was as well observed by the South Side, where lived most of the followers of handicrafts, as it was upon National Avenue and Amberson Boulevard. The perambulators of these two thoroughfares were the more luxurious; fine lace was to be seen upon the occupants, and the accompanying parents were well dressed; though Lena, looking from her window, sometimes shivered to see one of the passing young husbands wearing a Derby hat as a complement to the long frock coat that appeared to be a regalia garment necessary to this occasion.

By four o’clock, which was Dan’s favourite hour for his weekly perambulator stroll, most of the pedestrian families were on their way homeward from “Sunday dinner at grandpa’s and grandma’s,” the grandma and grandpa being almost invariably the parents of the young mother. Lena objected to the parade as “publicly provincial,” and pointed out that Dan lacked any plausible reason for joining it;—if the baby needed air he could be taken for a drive in the family carriage; and if Dan insisted on pushing him in the perambulator, the Oliphants’ back yard was “twice the size of Madison Square,” she said with elaborate exaggeration; but Henry Daniel’s father only laughed and continued to follow the custom of his fellow-townsmen.

The Sunday-afternoon excursion with the perambulator gave him his greatest happiness, and all through his bustling week days of work he looked forward to it, chuckling as he thought of it. And when the rewarding hour arrived, he went forth wheeling his son before him and cheerily unconscious that he was the only father in sight not accompanied (even at a distance) by a second parent for the occupant of the perambulator. He was proud to exhibit Henry Daniel and loved nothing better than to lift him out of the little carriage and talk uproarious babytalk to him, and tickle him to make him laugh, and in every other possible manner show him off to other young parents—or to anybody who had time to listen to these hilarious paternal banalities. If other parents bragged of their own young, showing them off in turn, Dan’s manifestations with Henry Daniel would become but the louder; and if the other parents, being two to one, succeeded in drowning him out, he would restore his child to the perambulator tenderly and move on, sorry for people who had so little to make such a fuss about.

===Chinese===

她一心扑在娃娃身上，或许正是在这份专注里寻着一处避难所；可每逢星期天下午丹推着婴儿车（perambulator）上人行道去遛弯，她一概不肯奉陪。她指出，这是城里一条定了型的风俗：每到星期天，午后不久，年轻的父亲母亲们便开始在人行道上露面，父亲们推着婴儿车，母亲们缀在后头不远，牵着蹒跚学步的孩子——要是有的话——或者半路碰上熟人，站住闲扯几句，然后又急急赶上去，追上那辆婴儿车。

这风俗上下通行；城南那边住的大半是靠手艺吃饭的人家，守起这风俗来，同国民大道（National Avenue）和安伯森大道（Amberson Boulevard）上一样一丝不苟。这两条通衢大道上的婴儿车要考究些：车里的乘客看得见细细的花边，随行的父母也衣履鲜明；不过莉娜有时从窗口望下去，看见过路的年轻丈夫里有人戴一顶圆顶礼帽（Derby hat），去配那件长常礼服——在这场合，那衣服似乎是一件少不了的礼典正装——会禁不住打一个寒噤。

到四点钟——那是丹每星期推婴儿车遛弯最中意的时辰——大多数遛弯的人家，都从“外公外婆家吃星期天饭”的席面上往家转了，那外公外婆，差不多无一例外是年轻母亲一方的爹娘。莉娜嫌这场游行“乡气得招摇过市”，还指出丹根本没有一个说得过去的理由去凑这份热闹；——娃娃要是需要透气，坐自家的马车出去兜一圈就是了；丹要是非推婴儿车不可，奥利芬特家的后院“比麦迪逊广场（Madison Square）还大一倍”，她挖空心思地夸张道；可亨利·丹尼尔的父亲只是哈哈一笑，照旧遵从他同城街坊们的风俗。

星期天下午推着婴儿车的这趟远足，是他顶大的乐事；整个星期忙忙碌碌的工作日里，他一想到它就盼着，想起便独自吃吃地笑。等那有犒赏的时辰一到，他便推着儿子出门，儿子在他前头的车里；他欢欢喜喜，浑然不觉放眼望去只有他一位父亲，身边没有第二位家长（哪怕远远地）陪着婴儿车里那位小乘客。他以炫耀亨利·丹尼尔为荣，顶爱把他从小车里抱出来，同他哇啦哇啦讲娃娃话，咯吱他，逗他笑，千方百计把他显摆给别的年轻父母看——或者显摆给任何一个有工夫听这些当爹的乐癫癫老生常谈的人听。若是别家父母夸自家的崽子，轮番地显摆，丹同亨利·丹尼尔的这一番表演只会愈发响亮；而若别家父母二对一，硬把他的声音压了下去，他便温柔地把孩子放回婴儿车里，推着车往前走，心里替他们惋惜——值得这样大呼小叫的东西，他们实在少得可怜。

===Original===

Sunday, he said, was the only day when he had a chance to get really acquainted with the baby; for all the rest of the week Dan was out hustling so early and so late that opportunities for making the acquaintance more intimate were few. A great part of his activity at this time was in the chase of possible buyers of Ornaby ground; and a driven life was led by those three men who had thought they might buy lots after the foreclosure. The Earl of Ornaby gave them little rest; and although he sometimes remained away from one or another of them for days at a time—perhaps upon the ardent request, “Well, for heaven’s sakes can’t you even give me a chance to think it *over*?”—he would write frequent letters to the pursued creature in the interval. Incessantly he persuaded, argued, and prophesied; seldom has a half-accepted, half-rejected lover shown such hot persistence in convincing his lady; and probably never have three dismal men in moderate circumstances been so urgently courted into the buying of lots.

They were not friends, these men; they had gone separately to Ornaby and had no knowledge of one another when the pursuit of them began; but they knew one another well before it was over. The vehement salesman had so quoted them to one another, making such glorifying use of their every admission not actually condemning Ornaby, that a conference of the quoted seemed to be a necessity. They thought to meet in secret; but within ten minutes found the hunter upon them.

“Gentlemen,” he began, “you wish to be alone, and I will not interrupt you”—and talked until two of them went home.

He went with them, and then returned to talk some more to the man at whose house the conference had been called.

===Chinese===

他说，星期天是他唯一有机会同娃娃真正混熟的日子；因为一星期里其余的日子，丹出门都起早贪黑地奔波，要把交情处得更深一层的工夫实在难得。他这阵子的奔走，大半花在追猎奥纳比地皮的可能买主上；那三位原以为取消赎回权过后能买上几块地的人，日子过得就像被人驱赶着一般。奥纳比伯爵（Earl of Ornaby）不给他们片刻安宁；虽说有时他也会一连几天不去搅扰其中某一位——多半是应了对方火辣辣的哀告：“哎，老天爷，你就不能给我个机会，让我把这事*好好想一想*吗？”——可就在这间隙里，他还会勤勤地给那位被追猎的人写信。他劝说、辩论、预言，无一刻停歇；半推半就、欲拒还迎的情人里头，很少有几个像他这样火烧火燎地非要说服自己那位小姐不可的；而三个境况平平、愁眉苦脸的人被这样十万火急地像求爱一般劝去买地皮，恐怕也是开天辟地头一遭。

这几位并不是朋友；他们是各自分头上的奥纳比，追逐开始的时候彼此谁也不认识谁；可到这场追逐收场之前，他们彼此已经熟透了。那位气势汹汹的推销员把他们你一句我一句地互相引用，凡他们口风中还不算干脆贬低奥纳比的每一点承认，都被他拿来镀金增光，以致被引用的几位觉得，不开一次当面对证的会简直不行了。他们想偷偷地聚一聚；可不到十分钟，那猎人已经扑到他们头上。

“二位先生，”他开言道，“你们想单独谈谈，我就不打扰了”——跟着便谈了起来，直谈到其中两位回家去了。

他还陪着那两位走了一程，然后折回来，接着同在自家召集这次会议的那一位又谈了一大阵。

===Original===

Such deadly persistence finally prevailed upon a majority of the three and two more lots were sold in the Addition upon the liberal terms of nothing down and little more than nothing to be paid in periodical installments. Nevertheless, here were three actual sales, and if there ever lived a salesman who knew how to make three appear to be a hundred, because he himself believed three to be a hundred, that multi-visioned salesman was Daniel Oliphant.

In a day of quieter art certain academicians now gone from their academies had frequently the desire to paint pretty young women blue-robed and poised as if alighting from the air. Sometimes, upon the lower part of his canvas, beneath the poising lady’s alighting toe, such a painter would twirl a golden circle, then swathe her eyes with a blue kerchief and name the picture, “Dame Fortune on her Wheel.” The effect was of the dame blind, but dancing; and sometimes the course of events in the life of a human creature will warrant the conception, yet it has usually been observed that Fortune seldom dances to one who has not diligently begged the favour. It would seem the blinded lady has a little bit of her kerchief up.

The man who had built a picnic shack at Ornaby for his large family found his wife and children so reluctant to come home from the picnics that he enlarged the shack, put a cooking-stove and cots in it, and began to stay there from Saturday afternoon until Monday morning. His house was far down in the city where the smoke had begun to discourage his wife, and, in the unavailing struggle to keep things clean, she grew querulous. “If we could only *live* out here!” she wailed one day when they were at the shack; and this outcry produced the first house in Ornaby Addition. It was a cottage of the “New Colonial” kind; and Dan drove all of his other Ornaby boosters to see every new phase of its construction, from the digging of the cellar to the polishing of the floors; for when the cottage was begun the purchasers of land in the Addition were increased in number to eight. By the time the cottage was finished there were fourteen, and several of these intended to build “right the first minute next spring,” Dan said.

He called them his “Ornaby boosters”; for he readily adopted the new vocabulary of commercial argot then being developed by promoters, by writers of advertisements, and by New York hustlers for trade. “Every Ornaby buyer is an Ornaby booster,” he said one day, when the new cottages in the Addition had brought him new buyers of lots; and, falling instantly in love with the cadence of this alliteration, went straight to the billboard men. Thereafter no one could go northward of the city for an afternoon drive and fail to find the gentle landscape wrecked. On every road the earl blazoned his great defacements: “Every Ornaby Buyer is an Ornaby Booster!”

===Chinese===

这样要命的不依不饶，终于在那三位当中说动了过半数；新区里于是又卖出了两块地，条件宽厚无比：首期分文不付，往后按期该付的，也差不多等于不付。话虽如此，这总归是三笔实打实的买卖；而倘若世上果真有过一位推销员，懂得怎样叫三显得像一百——因为他自己真把三信成了一百——那么，这位眼界里自有百数的推销员，不是别人，正是丹尼尔·奥利芬特。

在画风还要安闲的年代，有几位如今早已不在他们那些学院里的学院派画师，常常一心想画漂亮的年轻女人：一袭蓝袍，悬停半空，仿佛正从天上翩然降下。这样的画师有时会在画布的下方、那悬停美人将落未落的足尖底下，描出一道金色的圆环，再用一方蓝巾蒙住她的两眼，给画题名《转轮上的命运夫人》（Dame Fortune on her Wheel）。画出来的寓意是：夫人虽然蒙着眼，却照样在起舞；人间世事有时确实当得起这个构想，然而人们通常注意到，不曾苦苦求过她赏光的人，命运夫人是难得与之共舞的。如今看来，这位蒙眼的夫人，蓝巾底下似乎悄悄掀起了一角。

有个人在奥纳比给他一大家子盖了一座野餐棚屋，后来发现老婆孩子野完了餐都赖着不肯回家，索性把棚屋扩建，里头安上一只炉灶、几张行军床，从星期六下午一直住到星期一早上。他的宅子在城那头的深处，烟尘已经开始叫他的妻子泄气；在那场叫家里干净、却总是白费气力的挣扎里，她渐渐变得爱发牢骚。“咱们要是能*住*在这儿该多好！”有一天在棚屋里她哀声叹道；这一声哀叹，就催出了奥纳比新区的第一座房子。那是一座“新殖民地式”（New Colonial）的小屋；丹拉上他其余那些奥纳比吹鼓手（Ornaby boosters），赶着车去看它施工的每一样新进展，从挖地窖一直看到磨地板；因为小屋动工的时候，新区购地的人已经增加到八位。等小屋落成，数目已是十四位；据丹说，其中好几位打算“明年开春头一分钟就动工”。

他管这些人叫他的“奥纳比吹鼓手”；因为当时开发商、广告文案和纽约那些拉生意的好手正在锻造一套新的商业行话，他欣然全盘搬用。“每一个奥纳比买家都是奥纳比吹鼓手，”新区里新起的小屋给他招来了新的购地人之后，有一天他这样说；这句头韵铿锵的调子，他一听就迷上了，当即径直去找竖广告牌的商家。从此，谁想出城往北兜个下午的风，就免不了撞见那一片温婉的乡野风光横遭蹂躏。条条大路上，伯爵都在张扬他的伟大毁损：“每一个奥纳比买家都是奥纳比吹鼓手！”

===Original===

At home he had two subjects, both subdivided. One was Henry Daniel, his growth, his wit, and his precocity; and the other was Ornaby Addition, its present magnificence and prospective splendour.

“And the queer thing is,” Harlan told Martha Shelby, “he believes every word of it. He actually still believes he’s making a success of that dreadful place. Isn’t it strange?”

But Martha said that she knew something stranger, and when he asked her what it was, she answered: “Why, it’s your still believing he *isn’t* making a success of the ‘dreadful place.’ ”

===Chinese===

在家里，他有两个题目，各自又细分出许多小目。一个是亨利·丹尼尔：他的成长，他的机灵，他的早慧；另一个是奥纳比新区：它眼下的宏伟，和它将来的辉煌。

“顶古怪的是，”哈兰对玛莎·谢尔比说，“这里头他一个字都信。他到今天还真相信，他把那个可怕的地方经营成了一桩成功的事业。你说怪不怪？”

可玛莎说，她知道一件还要古怪的事；他问是什么，她答道：“嗐，就是你到今天还相信他*没*把那‘可怕的地方’经营成什么成功事业呀。”

## XIX / 十九

===Original===

Harlan laughed ruefully and told her that time, tide, and travel failed to alter her. “You don’t change as much as—as much as”—he looked about him for a comparison, and found one ready to hand in the material of which the Shelbys’ veranda was made. “You don’t change as much as this Western limestone does. It’s made of stone, too, but years and weather take its edges off and give it the look of being not so hard as it used to be.”

Not defending herself from the criticism, she gazed thoughtfully at Harlan as he sat fanning himself with his straw hat—he was warm and flushed after their walk on this hot June morning—then she turned her eyes again to the wide lawn stretching before her down to the National Avenue sidewalk. Looking out from the shade of the veranda, her eyes needed the shelter of the curved fingers of her hand, a protection she gave them, resting her elbow on the stone railing beside her. The trimmed grass of the lawn was a blazing green, seen waveringly through visible pulsations of the heated air; the fountain swan, still diligent under every discouragement, sprayed forth no skyward rainbow mists, but ejected a limpid rod of water of so brief an uplift that the bird seemed to carry in his throat the curved tip of a shepherd’s crook made of glass. The asphalt street, beyond the shade of its bordering maples, lay steaming and smelled of tar;—drooping bicyclists rode there, tinkling their little bells for a right of way. Surreys and phaetons gave them courteous passage, and frequently a swifter, noisier vehicle went by, grinding, squawking, and leaving blue oil-smoke on the air.

There were many more automobiles than when she had last gone away, Martha noticed; yet the outlook from the veranda was the old familiar one. To her eyes, however, it bore the familiar unfamiliar appearance that well-known things bear to the traveller at home again, but not yet quite adjusted after a long absence. For this was not her return from the little run she had made to England at the time of the baby’s christening next door, though that excursion was itself a longer one—much to her taste—than she had planned. The bit of old hickory serving her as a father resisted stiffly, but finally proved flexible under great pressure, and she took him even to Russia before she got through bending him. When his protestive squeakings at last became unbearable, she brought him home, but did not remain herself. In the Italian Alps there was a valley town with which she had fallen in love;—she returned to her native land merely as an escort for Mr. Shelby, and, having deposited him safely, hurried back to the terraced vineyards, the whitewashed walls with strings of red peppers dangling against them, and the frescoed old villa she had rented in the foreground of this picture.

===Chinese===

哈兰（Harlan）苦笑着对她说，光阴、潮水，加上旅行，都改变不了她。“你变得还不如——还不如”——他四下张望，想找一个比方，结果眼前就现成有一个，正是谢尔比（Shelby）家这道游廊用的石料。“你变得还不如这西部的石灰石呢。它也是石头做的，可年深日久、风吹雨打，把它的棱角磨去了，看上去就不像从前那么硬了。”

她并不为自己分辩，只是若有所思地望着哈兰——六月里一个炎热的上午，两人刚散完步，他正热得满脸通红，坐在那儿拿草帽给自己扇风；随后她把目光移回眼前那片宽大的草坪，草坪一路铺展到国民大道（National Avenue）的人行道边。从游廊的荫凉里望出去，她的眼睛需要弯起的手指来遮一遮，她便给了它们这份庇护，手肘支在身旁的石栏杆上。修剪过的草坪绿得灼人，透过热空气里肉眼可见的搏动，那绿色微微地晃；喷泉上的天鹅在种种挫败之下依旧勤勉，却喷不出一蓬升天生虹的水雾，只吐出一根清亮的水柱，扬得那样短，那鸟儿仿佛喉咙里衔着一柄玻璃牧羊杖的弯头。路旁成行枫树的荫凉之外，柏油街面蒸腾着热气，散发出焦油的气味——蔫头耷脑的骑车人在路上走，摇着小铃铛讨一条路权。萨里式游览车（surreys）和四轮敞车（phaetons）客客气气地给他们让道，而更快、更吵的车辆也频频驶过，磨着牙、嘎嘎叫着，把青蓝色的油烟留在空气里。

玛莎（Martha）注意到，汽车比她上回离开的时候多了许多；可是游廊外的景致，依旧是那副熟悉的旧模样。然而在她眼里，它带着一种既熟悉又陌生的神气——久别归来的游子乍一看家中旧物，都是这样，人还没完全缓过神来。因为这回她并不是从隔壁小宝贝受洗时那趟英格兰（England）小旅行归来——虽说那趟远游本身也比她原计划长了不少，很合她的口味。那块给她当父亲的老山核桃木料起先硬邦邦地顶着，到底经不住大力，显出了韧性；她把他一路掰到了俄国（Russia）才罢休。等到他抗议的吱嘎声终于叫人忍无可忍，她把他送回家来，自己却没有留下。意大利阿尔卑斯山（Italian Alps）里有一座山谷小城，她爱上了那里——她回故国纯粹是为了护送谢尔比先生（Mr. Shelby），把他稳稳当当安顿好，便又奔回那层层叠叠的葡萄园、刷白的墙上悬着的一串串红辣椒，还有她租下的那座老别墅——四壁有湿壁画，正立在这幅图画的前景上。

===Original===

It was a commonplace, she said, that the new Twentieth Century was the age of the annihilation of distances; people talked from New York to Chicago over a wire; the Atlantic was crossed in six days, the American continent in four; and her father could remember when it took him three weeks to get to Philadelphia; he “wouldn’t mind being taken care of by correspondence.”

Old Hickory, well-warranted in his outburst, replied that he didn’t “need any takin’ care of, thank you”—he was tired of being bossed to death, and he wanted her to understand she was mighty welcome to go and stay as long as she had a mind to! If she remained at home, he wouldn’t know when she might be draggin’ him off again without his exactly knowin’ how it happened. It was “curious,” he continued; he had sense enough never to let her interfere with him in his business; but in other matters he never knew when he mightn’t find himself in some dog-gone place he didn’t *want* to be in—at a plague-taken pink tea maybe, or even right spang in the middle of Europe in some heaven-forsaken garlic heap, with nothin’ to think about but old dead monks and nothin’ to do but hate the smell. If Martha liked hangin’ around those old worn-out nations that never showed a sign o’ life except advertisin’ chocolate and keepin’ their fertilizer right under their front parlour window for fear somebody’d steal it, why, she was certainly good and welcome to all she wanted of ’em! For himself, he had his business to ’tend to; and he didn’t want any aunt Ella to pester him, either; “aunt Ella” being his widowed sister, whom Martha had proposed as a housekeeper in substitute for herself. He was full and able—thank you again!—to get up in the morning and eat his ham and eggs without somebody’s pinning a bib around his neck, and he believed he knew how to wash and go to bed at night without any fussy woman fixin’ up his bureau every other day, so as to hide his nightshirts from him! Altogether, he was lookin’ forward to a little rest and liberty, thank you!

So Martha had gone with his earnest consent; for his complaint of her did not lack reason—she was headstrong and a compelling daughter—and she stayed until she had her fill of Italy for that while. Meantime, the abandoned father contentedly lived alone, except for his negro servants, and declared that at last he was his own man and began to feel as if he owned his own house; he felt that way for the first time since his daughter was born, he said. But a different view of his condition was maintained by a member of the household next door.

===Chinese===

她说，人人都念叨，崭新的二十世纪乃是一个消灭距离的时代：从纽约（New York）到芝加哥（Chicago），人们隔着一根电线就能说话；横渡大西洋要六天，横穿美洲大陆要四天；而她父亲还记得，他当年去一趟费城（Philadelphia）要走三个星期，他说他“倒不介意让人靠书信来照料”。

“老山核桃”这一通发作倒是事出有因，他回敬说，他“用不着谁来照料，多谢关心”——他叫人管得要死，早就烦透了；他要她明白，她要走、要走多久，他都再欢迎不过！她要是待在家里，他可说不准哪天又会稀里糊涂地被她拖走，连怎么回事都没弄明白。他接着说，这才叫“怪”；生意上的事他有脑子，从来不让她插手；可在别的事情上，他说不准什么时候就会发现自己落进了什么鬼地方，他*根本不想去*——也许是遭瘟的粉红茶会，也许干脆一屁股坐在欧洲正当中哪个上帝遗弃的蒜臭堆子里，满脑子除了死了千年的老修士没别的可想，除了恨那股味儿没别的可干。玛莎要是喜欢在那些老掉了牙的破国家里泡着——它们除了给巧克力做广告、把肥料就堆在自家前客厅窗底下怕人偷走，再显不出半点活气——那好，她想要多少都尽管拿去，请便！他自有生意要照料；他也不要哪个艾拉姑妈（aunt Ella）来絮叨他——“艾拉姑妈”是他守寡的姐姐，玛莎提议由她来顶自己管家婆的缺。他精神足、本事有——再次道谢！——早上起得了床，吃得下火腿鸡蛋，用不着谁往他脖子上系围嘴；他相信自己知道怎么洗脸、晚上怎么上床睡觉，用不着哪个多事婆娘隔三差五收拾他的五斗柜，把他的睡衣都藏起来不让他找着！总而言之，他正盼着过几天清静自在的日子，多谢关心！

于是玛莎便在他一片至诚的同意之下走了；他数落她的那些话并非全无道理——她性子倔，又是个强势逼人的女儿——她一直待到那一阵子把意大利看够了才算完。这期间，被撇下的父亲倒自得其乐地独居着，除了几个黑人仆人再无旁人，还宣称他终于做了自己的主人，开始觉得这座房子像是自己的了；他说，自打女儿出生以来，他头一回有这种感觉。可是在隔壁那户人家，有一位成员对他的境况另有一番看法。

===Original===

“A fine exhibition of filial duty!” Lena cried, in one of the irritated moods that returned upon her as the growing Henry Daniel began to be a little boy instead of a little baby. When he was a noisy little boy during the day his mother often became reminiscent, not happily, by the time his father came home in the evening. “You told me once she had a heart as big as she was,” Lena went on. “It looks like it, doesn’t it? Leaving that poor old man alone over there, month after month and year after year!”

Dan listened absently, his mind on a new customer for a lot. “Who you talkin’ about now?”

“You know! That big girl of yours.”

“Martha?” he said, his tone a weary one instantly. “How often have I told you she never was any girl of mine, big or little? What’s started you on *that* again?”

===Chinese===

“好一出孝道典范！”莉娜（Lena）嚷道。随着亨利·丹尼尔（Henry Daniel）渐渐长大，从小婴儿变成了小男孩，这种恼怒的心绪便时时回到她身上。白天他是个吵吵闹闹的小男孩，到晚上父亲回家的时候，他母亲往往就陷入回忆，而且不是愉快的回忆。“你从前跟我说过，她的心眼儿跟她的个头儿一样大，”莉娜接着说。“看来是这样，是不是？把那可怜的老头儿一个人撂在那边，一月又一月，一年又一年！”

丹（Dan）心不在焉地听着，脑子里想的是一块地皮的新买主。“你这又说谁呢？”

“你知道！你那个大姑娘。”

“玛莎？”他说，声调顿时透出疲惫来。“我跟你说过多少回了，她从来就不是我的什么姑娘，大的小的都不是？又是什么让你*想起这个*来了？”

===Original===

“I shouldn’t think you’d expect it would take much to start me,” Lena exclaimed, “when you remember you gave me your sacred promise I should have a year in Europe—”

“Oh, Lordy! Have we got to go all over that again?”

“—And when you remember you deliberately broke your word to me,” Lena went on, “and haven’t ever even made the slightest effort to keep it! You hold me here, suffocating in this place, year after year—”

“Now, see here,” he interrupted; “just think a minute, please! Is that fair? Haven’t you been back to New York every year for at least two or three—”

But Lena almost shouted her interruption. “Yes! Two or three *weeks*! To visit my *fam*ily! Do you think it means happiness for me to be with *them*?—and all of ’em watching to see how I take care of my baby! Is that keeping your word to take me abroad? Oh,” she cried, with bitter laughter, “doesn’t it seem ironical even to you? That big creature next door was so jealous of me because I had what she wanted she couldn’t bear to stay where she had to look at it, so she goes away and gets what *I* wanted! *Isn’t* it ironical, Dan? Don’t you see it at all?”

===Chinese===

“我想你总不至于以为，勾起我这个来要多大点儿事吧，”莉娜嚷道，“你只要想想，你郑重其事地答应过我，让我去欧洲待上一年——”

“哦，老天爷！这套又得从头来一遍吗？”

“——再想想你是怎么蓄意对我食言的，”莉娜续道，“连守住诺言的一丁点儿努力都不曾做过！你把我拴在这儿，困在这个地方透不过气来，一年又一年——”

“哎，你听我说，”他插进来，“劳驾先想上一分钟！这公平吗？你哪一年没回纽约，一待至少两三个——”

可莉娜几乎嚷了起来，打断了他。“对！两三个*星期*！去看我的*家*人！你以为我跟*他们*待在一起是幸福吗？——他们一个个都盯着瞧我怎么带我的孩子！这就算守了你带我出国的话吗？哦，”她带着苦笑嚷道，“这难道连你都不觉得讽刺吗？隔壁那个大块头，当初嫉妒我，就因为我有她想要的东西，她连待在不得不看上一眼的地方都不能忍，只好走开；如今她出了门，倒把我*想要*的东西弄到手了！这*还不*讽刺吗，丹？你难道一点儿都看不出来？”

===Original===

“I see you’ve got your imagination all stirred up again, that’s all.”

“Imagination!” she cried. “Yes; I should think my imagination *would* get ‘all stirred up!’ Why, it’s funny! She can go and take what I want, but it can’t be any good to her; she hasn’t culture enough to see it or to feel it or to hear it. I can see her carrying that accent around Europe, and asking waiters for ‘ice wat-urr’ and ‘please to pass the but-urr!’ Yet she can go and I can’t!”

“But *I* didn’t send her,” Dan explained, since his wife clearly implied his responsibility. “You talk as if I—”

“No; but you had no right not to send *me* after giving me your sacred—”

===Chinese===

“我看是你的想象力又翻腾起来了，就是这么回事。”

“想象力！”她嚷道。“是啊，我的想象力*当然*要‘翻腾起来’了！哼，真可笑！她能出门，把我想要的东西拿走，可那东西到她手里也白搭；她那点教养，看也看不出，感也感不到，听也听不见。我都看见她在欧洲带着那口音到处走，跟侍者要‘冰瓦特*儿*’，还要‘劳驾把巴特*儿*递过来’！她去得，我就去不得！”

“可送她去的又不是*我*，”丹解释道，因为妻子显然话里话外都认定是他的责任。“听你这口气，倒好像是我——”

“是，可你郑重其事地许下了我——事到临头倒不送*我*去，你没这个权利——”

===Original===

Dan interrupted her genially; he smiled and patted her pretty little shoulder, though it twitched away from his touch. “Lena, look here: I’ve got some big deals on, and I’m just about certain they’re goin’ to work out the right way. You see up to now the trouble’s been that all the money comin’ in had to be put right out again almost before I’d get hold of it. If it hadn’t been for that, I’d had that factory up and running long ago. But as I look ahead now, everything is mighty good—*mighty* good! If I can just put these deals through—”

“Yes; it’s always ‘if,’ ” she reminded him. “When have I ever talked to you that you weren’t just about to put through some ‘mighty big deals’? You said exactly the same last year.”

“Well, but this is a better year than last year. Why, I’ve done twice the business—yes, better’n that; it’s more like four times what I did last year. If Ornaby keeps on like this, why, a few years from now—”

She stopped him; informing him that she’d long since heard more than enough about “a few years from now”; whereupon, being full of the subject, he went down to the library to tell his father and mother what was inevitable within a few years. No skepticism dampened his library prophecies now; Harlan was no longer there to listen, staring with dry incredulity through his glasses.

===Chinese===

丹和颜悦色地打断了她；他微笑着拍了拍她那只漂亮的小肩膀，虽然肩膀从他掌下一扭便躲开了。“莉娜，你听我说：我手上正做着几笔大买卖，我差不多有十成把握，它们都会顺顺当当地成。你要明白，直到眼下，麻烦就在于进来的钱还没捂热，几乎就又得悉数投出去。要不是这样，那家工厂我早建起来开工了。可如今往前一看，样样都是好得很——*好得很*！只要这几笔买卖能成——”

“是啊，永远是‘只要’，”她提醒他。“我几回跟你说话，你不是正要把哪几笔‘大买卖’办成？去年你说的一字不差。”

“话是这么说，可今年比去年强。嘿，我做的生意翻了一番——不，还不止；差不多是去年的四倍。奥纳比要是照这样下去，那么再过几年——”

她止住了他，告诉他“再过几年”这套话她早就听得不能再多了；于是他满肚子还是这个题目，便下楼到藏书室，去向父母宣讲几年之内必然到来的一切。如今再没有怀疑来给他的藏书室预言泼冷水了；哈兰已经不在，不会再戴着他那副眼镜，干巴巴地满脸狐疑地坐在那里听了。

===Original===

Harlan had not sold Mrs. Savage’s old house, but had moved into it, and kept as precise a routine there as she had kept, and with the same servants. He had two bedrooms upstairs made into a library, but changed nothing on the lower floor; and often the old lady seemed still to be there in authority. At twilight, before Nimbus lit the electric table lamp in the “south front parlour,” the room to which she had always descended from her afternoon nap, it was not difficult to imagine that she was sitting in the stiff chair beside the plate-glass window. Of course Nimbus believed that he saw her there when he came in to light the lamp; and he often mumbled to her—always upon the same theme. He was grateful for the one hundred and thirty-five dollars she had left him, but considered the sum inadequate.

“No’m, indeed,” he said to the figure he saw in the stiff chair. “I thank you kindly, but didn’ I used you right all my days? How much it cost you slip down ten hunderd thirty-five on that paper, ’stead of one hunderd thirty-five? *You* ain’t got it, are you? Ain’t doin’ *you* no good, do it? No’m, indeedy! ’Tain’t no use you bein’ sorry, neither. Make all the fuss you want to; you too late; nobody ain’t goin’ pay no ’tention to you!”

And in the kitchen he would discuss the apparition with his fourth wife, the fat cook, Myrtle. “Look to me like she can’t keep away,” he would say. “Set there same as ever. Set up straight in that stiff chair. See her plain as I see you, till I git that lamp lit.”

“Landy me, Nimbus, I wouldn’ go in that room unlessen the light bright as day if you give me trottin’ horse an’ gole harniss! How you keep from hollerin’?”

===Chinese===

萨维奇老太太（Mrs. Savage）的旧宅，哈兰没有卖掉，而是搬了进去，起居作息同她当年一样一丝不苟，仆人用的还是原班。他把楼上两间卧室打通改成藏书室，楼下一层却什么也没动；那位老太太常常仿佛仍在那里当着家。黄昏时分，尼姆巴斯（Nimbus）还未进“南前客厅”去点台灯——那是老太太午睡之后照例要下楼来的屋子——不难想象她就坐在厚玻璃窗旁那把硬背椅上。尼姆巴斯进来点灯时，自然相信自己看见了她；他常常对着她咕咕哝哝——翻来覆去总是同一个题目。她留给他的一百三十五美元，他是感激的，只是嫌这笔数目太小。

“是的，太太，半点不假，”他对硬背椅上那个人影说。“我诚心诚意谢您，可我这一辈子哪点儿亏待过您？您在纸上把一千零三十五滑成了一百三十五，这一下亏了您多少？*您*自个儿得不着它了，是吧？得不着，对*您*没半点好处，是不是？是的，太太，半点不假！您后悔也白搭。您尽管闹腾去；太晚啦；没谁打算理您这一套！”

到了厨房，他就跟第四任妻子、胖厨娘玛特尔（Myrtle）议论那个幽灵。“瞧着像是她离不开这儿了，”他总这么说。“老样子坐在那儿。在那把硬背椅里坐得笔直。灯点着以前，我看她看得同看你一样清楚。”

“老天爷呐，尼姆巴斯，你就是送我一匹快步马、配一副金马具，除非灯亮得跟大白天一样，要不我才不进那间屋呢！你怎么能忍住不喊出声来？”

===Original===

At this the tall, thin old fellow would laugh without making a sound; deep wrinkles in the design of half of a symmetrical cobweb appearing on each side of his face. Some profoundly interior secret of his might have been betrayed, it seemed, if he had allowed his merriment to become vocal; and this noiseless laugh of his awed his wife in much the same way, no doubt, that the laugh of a jungle witch-doctor ancestor of his had awed wives not unlike Myrtle. “She ain’t goin’ bother me ner you,” he explained. “She ain’t settin’ there ’count o’ me ner you. She settin’ there ’cause she so mad.”

“Who? Who she mad at?”

“Mad at somep’m!” Nimbus would say, and, becoming less uncomfortably mystic might allow a human chuckle to escape him. “Set there mad long as she want to; ’tain’t goin’ do her no good. She ain’t *fixed* to make no changes now!”

===Chinese===

听了这话，那瘦高条儿的老头便不出声地笑起来；两边脸上现出深深的皱纹，纹路恰是半张对称蛛网的图样。看来他要是把心里的欢喜放出声来，说不定就会泄露某个藏得极深的秘密；而他这种无声的笑叫他妻子敬畏，那缘故，想必同他丛林里巫医祖先的笑声震慑住那些与玛特尔相差无几的妻子们时一模一样。“她不会来搅扰我，也不会搅扰你，”他解释说。“她坐在那儿，不为我也不是为了你。她坐在那儿，是因为她火气大。”

“谁？她跟谁怄气？”

“总有她怄的事儿！”尼姆巴斯会说，随后那股叫人不大自在的神秘劲儿淡了下来，也许会漏出一声凡人的咯咯笑。“她爱怄多久就怄多久；于事无补。她如今这副光景*铁定*了，什么也变不了啦！”

===Original===

The new owner lived in the old house almost as quietly as Mrs. Savage, in the visions of Nimbus, went on living there. Harlan had several times thought of going to Italy, but the idea never culminated in action.

“I wanted to come, though,” he told Martha, as they sat on her veranda that hot morning, the day after her return. “I wanted to more than I ever wanted to do anything else. You see I’ve almost stopped going to the office; I just dangle about there sometimes to please father, but I don’t care to practise law. It’s a silly way of spending one’s life after all, fighting the sordid disputes of squabbling people. There was really nothing to keep me here.”

She did not alter her attitude, but still looked out upon the old familiar unfamiliar scene from beneath her sheltering curved fingers. “If you wanted to come, why didn’t you?”

“Because I’d only have done it to see you, and I suppose I have a remnant of pride. If you’d like a better answer, think of what I told you about yourself. I didn’t come because I know you’re stony. I knew you hadn’t changed.”

===Chinese===

新主人在这座旧宅里过着日子，安静得同尼姆巴斯幻象中继续住在那里的萨维奇老太太几乎不相上下。哈兰几度动过去意大利的念头，可那念头从未落实成行。

“不过我是想来着，”她回来的第二天，那个炎热的上午，他同她坐在她家的游廊上，对她这样说。“我想来，想得比这辈子想做过的任何事都切。你知道，我差不多已经不去事务所了；有时为了敷衍父亲，才去那儿晃一晃，可我无心操持律师这一行。说到底，把一辈子花在调解吵吵嚷嚷的人那些腌臜纠纷上，实在是桩傻事。这里实在没有什么东西留住我。”

她姿势未改，依旧从弯起遮光的手指底下，望着那片熟悉而又陌生的旧景致。“你想来，怎么没来？”

“因为我来只有一个目的，就是见你，而我大约还剩下一星半点的自尊。你要想听个更中听的答复，就想想我从前说你的那些话。我没来，是因为我知道你心硬如石。我知道你没有变。”

===Original===

“About what?”

“About me,” he said, and added: “About anything!”

At this she turned her head and looked at him, for he spoke with a sour significance. “Well, have you changed, Harlan?” she asked gravely.

“About you,” he answered. “I haven’t—unfortunately.”

===Chinese===

“说过我什么？”

“说你，”他说，又添上一句：“说你的一切！”

听到这里，她转过头来望着他，因为他说得又酸，又意有所指。“那么，你变了没有，哈兰？”她郑重地问。

“关于你么，”他答道，“我没有变——遗憾的是，没变。”

===Original===

“But I meant: Have *you* changed about anything? Aren’t you just what you were five or six years ago, only a little intensified—and richer?”

“Ah, I knew I’d get that,” he said. “I knew it would come before I could be with you long. I told my father and mother the very day my grandmother’s will was read that you’d hate me for it, and mother agreed quickly enough.”

“Why, no,” Martha said, and her surprise was genuine. “Why should I hate you because Mrs. Savage—”

“Because she left it to me and not to Dan, and because I didn’t think it was right or sensible to help him with any of it.”

===Chinese===

“可我的意思是：*你*自个儿变过没有，不管哪一方面？你不还是五六年前那个人么，只是变得更厉害了些——也更阔了些？”

“啊，我就知道会等来这一句，”他说。“我就知道，同你处不了一阵子，它准会来。外祖母的遗嘱宣读那一天，我就对我父母说过，你会为这件事恨我，母亲立刻就附和了。”

“哟，没有啊，”玛莎说，她的诧异是真的。“我为什么要恨你呢，就因为萨维奇老太太——”

“就因为她把遗产留给了我而不是丹，也因为我认为，拿这笔钱里的一个子儿去帮他是既不对路、也不明智。”

===Original===

“But he hasn’t needed any help,” Martha said. “It’s much better for him to be doing it without any help, and so splendidly.”

“So splendidly?” Harlan repeated, and he stared at her. “But you don’t take what Dan says seriously, do you? You don’t think that just because he says—”

“I haven’t seen him, Harlan.”

“But you speak as if you believe he’s actually succeeding in making that old fantasia of his into a reality.”

“Well,” she said, “isn’t he?”

===Chinese===

“可他并不需要谁帮忙，”玛莎说。“他不靠任何帮衬来做这件事，要好得多，而且做得这么辉煌。”

“这么辉煌？”哈兰重复了一遍，眼睛直盯着她。“可你不会把丹说的话当真吧？你总不至于因为他嘴上那么说——”

“我没见过他，哈兰。”

“可你说起话来，倒好像你真相信他正在把他那出老幻想曲变成现实似的。”

“哦，”她说，“难道不是么？”

===Original===

“What? Why, he’s still just barely keeping his head above water. He sells vacant lots out there, yes—but to keep on selling them he has to put all they sell for into developing the land he hasn’t sold. It amounts precisely to the same thing as giving the property away. His mortgages used to worry him to death, but he’s got most of the place mortgaged now for three times what it was five years ago. You see—”

“I see that the land must be worth three times as much as it was five years ago, since he can borrow three times as much on it.”

“But, my dear Martha—”

“But, my dear Harlan!” she echoed mockingly, and thus disposed of his argument before he could deliver it. “The truth is, you’ve had the habit of undervaluing Dan so long that you can’t get over it. You can’t see that at last he’s begun to make a success of his ‘fantasia.’ Given time enough, critics who aren’t careful to keep themselves humble-minded always lose the power to see things as they are.”

===Chinese===

“什么？嘿，他到如今也不过是勉强把脑袋浮在水面上罢了。他确实在往那儿卖空地——可为了能接着卖，卖地得来的钱他就得几乎原封不动地全部投进还没卖掉的地皮的开发里去。这说到底，同把产业白送人是一码事。从前那些抵押逼得他半死，如今那地方的大部分都已经押出去了，押到的数目是五年前的三倍。你瞧——”

“我瞧出来的是，那地皮准是值到五年前的三倍了，不然他怎么押得到三倍的钱。”

“可是，我亲爱的玛莎——”

“可是，我亲爱的哈兰！”她学着他的腔调讥讽道，不等他把道理摆出来，就先把它打发掉了。“实话说，你低估丹的习惯年头太久了，自己都改不掉。你看不出来，他终于把他那出‘幻想曲’做出了名堂。只要时间够长，不肯时时留神保持谦卑之心的批评家，迟早总要丧失如实观看事物的眼力。”

===Original===

Harlan winced a little under this sententious assault, and laughed at himself for wincing; then explained his rather painful laughter. “It’s almost amusing to me to find myself still cowering away from your humble-minded criticisms of me—just as I used to, Martha!”

“Yes, I know it,” she admitted. “I hate myself for the way I talk to you, Harlan;—somehow you always make me smug and superior. I’m the foolish kind of person who’s always made critical by superior criticism—critical of the critic, I mean.”

“But I’m not more critical of Dan than other people are. Have you asked your father what he thinks of Ornaby now, for instance?”

“Yes, I asked him last night.”

===Chinese===

这一番格言式的攻伐叫哈兰微微一缩，他又笑自己竟会缩；随后他解释了这笑声里几分管不住的苦味。“有意思的是，我发现在你这些诲人以谦的批评面前，我还是照旧往后缩——真同从前一模一样，玛莎！”

“是啊，我知道，”她承认。“我恨自己同你说话的这副腔调，哈兰——不知怎么，你总有本事把我变得又自得又高人一等。我就是那种傻人：批评若是高明，我就要变得吹毛求疵——我是说，挑剔起批评的人来。”

“可我对丹并不比别人更苛刻。打个比方，你问过你父亲，他如今怎么看奥纳比么？”

“问过，昨天晚上问的。”

===Original===

“What does he think of it?”

“He thinks the same as I do,” she said. “He’s been compelled to recognize that it’s going to be a tremendous success.”

“Then he’s changed his mind since last week,” Harlan returned, somewhat discomfited. “He told me—”

“Oh, yes, I know,” she said. “He didn’t say he thought it would be a success. He said he thought the Addition idea was just as crazy as he ever did, and Dan Oliphant was the biggest fool in seven states, and the noisiest! Those were his words precisely, Harlan.”

“But you just told me—”

===Chinese===

“他怎么看？”

“他跟我看法一样，”她说。“他已经不得不承认，这件事准会大成。”

“那他打上星期以来可是转了念了，”哈兰回了一句，有点儿下不来台。“他对我说——”

“哦，对，我知道，”她说。“他没说他认为这件事会成。他说的是，新区这个念头照旧疯得跟从前一样，丹·奥利芬特是七州之内最大的傻子，还是最吵的一个！字字都是他的原话，哈兰。”

“可你刚才明明说——”

===Original===

“No,” she explained;—“you asked me what he *thought*. Do you suppose he’d admit to me that he ever made a business mistake? He knows perfectly well that he did make one when he refused to follow my advice and buy some of Dan’s stock when the poor boy was trying to finance his plan at the beginning. Papa confessed it absolutely.”

“He did?”

“Certainly,” she replied. “If he’d meant what he said he’d just have grunted it. Instead, he yelled it at me. With papa, that’s exactly the same as a perfectly open confession.”

Harlan shook his head, remaining more than doubtful of this interpretation. “So you believe if Dan tried now to organize a stock company for Ornaby—”

“They’d gobble it!” she said. “Papa especially! But he and others like him wouldn’t buy a single share when poor Dan went begging and peddling all over town; and now I’m glad they didn’t. It’s so much better for him to have done it alone.”

===Chinese===

“没有，”她解释道；“你问的是他*怎么想*。你以为他会向我承认他做生意走过眼么？他心里亮堂得很：那可怜的孩子当初想方设法给自己的计划筹钱，他不听我的劝，不肯买丹的一点股票，这个错他是铸成了。爸爸认这个错，是认到底的。”

“他认了？”

“当然，”她答道。“他那话若是没有意思，哼一声也就完了。他可是一嗓子吼给我的。在爸爸那里，这就同把话摊开了明说完全是一回事。”

哈兰摇了摇头，对这一番讲法仍是难以全信。“这么说，你相信丹要是如今试着为奥纳比组建一家股份公司——”

“他们会一口把它吞了！”她说。“爸爸抢头一个！可怜的丹当年满城告帮、挨门兜售的时候，他同他那路人可是一股也不肯买的；如今我倒庆幸他们没有买。他全凭自己干成这件事，要好得多。”

===Original===

“But, my dear,” Harlan insisted, not altogether without exasperation, “he *hasn’t* done it.”

“My dear,” she returned promptly; “he’s *going* to!”

“But, Martha—”

“Listen,” she said. “I’ll tell you something that you don’t understand, because you’ve been living here all along. When I went off to college, I spent the Christmas holidays visiting some Eastern girls, and papa didn’t see me for a whole year. Then he nearly fainted—I’d grown so! Yet I’d grown just as much the year before, but he never noticed it because I was living at home where he saw me every day. It’s the same way with a city like this, Harlan. I haven’t been here for so long that I can see the change. Everything is going to happen that Dan prophesied.”

===Chinese===

“可是，我亲爱的，”哈兰坚持道，恼意已按捺不住几分，“他*还没有*干成。”

“我亲爱的，”她立刻顶了回去，“他*就要*干成了！”

“可是，玛莎——”

“你听，”她说。“有一样东西你不明白，因为你一直住在这儿没挪过窝。我上大学去的时候，圣诞节假期到东部几个姑娘家里做客，整整一年爸爸没见着我。再见面他差点儿晕过去——我长那么高了！可前一年我长得一样多，他却毫无察觉，因为我住在家里，他天天都看见我。这样一座城市也是同一个道理，哈兰。我离开得太久，变化在我眼里就显了形。丹预言过的一切，都要应验。”

===Original===

She had spoken with gravity, but Harlan laughed, not impressed. “Yes, the boosters brag of the increase in population shown by the last census,” he said. “We’ve got a few thousand more Italians and Polish Jews and negroes, I suppose; and some new ugly factories and dwelling-houses of objectionable architecture. They’re beginning to build awful little shacks they call ‘bungalows,’ hurrying them up by the dozen. Is that the glorious cosmopolis of your hero’s prophecies, Martha? To my mind it’s only an extension of hideousness, and down where I live, in my grandmother’s old house, it’s getting so smoky in winter that the air is noxious—the whole town’s dirty, for that matter.”

“Yes,” she said. “Yesterday, as soon as I got here, I noticed that even in summer the air’s smokier than it used to be. I think the city was a cleaner place and a better-looking place when I went away. There’s the smoke, of course, and I’ve already seen how they’re beginning to tear old buildings down and put up bigger ones, and no building has any thought of having the slightest relation to the ones on each side of it. In a way, as you say, it’s getting hideous, though some of these long, wide streets are pleasant, even to a person who’s stayed in Europe too long perhaps—and National Avenue is really beautiful. I don’t know where except in towns like this you’ll find a long street of such big, solid, comfortable houses with green trees and clean lawns about them. This part of the town, at least, hasn’t changed; but a change has begun, and I believe it’s the growth—I think it’s the incredible growth that Dan predicted, Harlan. I think it’s begun.”

Again she had spoken gravely, though with a glinting look at him which had in it some hint of triumph, and piqued him.

===Chinese===

她这话说得郑重，哈兰却笑了，全不为所动。“是啊，吹鼓手（boosters）们正夸耀上回人口普查所显示的人口增长呢，”他说。“想必又添了几千意大利人、波兰犹太人和黑人；添了几家难看的新工厂，几所叫人看不入眼的住宅。他们开始造那种可怕的小棚子了，管它叫‘平房’，成打成打地赶工。这就是你那位英雄预言的光荣大都会么，玛莎？照我看，不过是丑陋在延伸罢了；还有我住的那一头，我外祖母的旧宅那里，一到冬天烟得空气都有毒——论脏，全城又何止一处。”

“是啊，”她说。“昨天一到这儿我就察觉，连夏天空气也比从前烟多了。我想我走的时候，这座城还干净些，也好看些。烟自然是烟，而且我已经看见了，他们开始拆老楼、盖更大的楼，哪一栋楼都压根没想过要同左右两邻有一丝一毫的呼应。从某一方面说，正如你讲的，是越来越丑了；不过这些又长又宽的街道有些还是很可人意，哪怕对一个在欧洲待得未免太久的人——而国民大道是真正美的。除了这样的城镇，我不知道还能在什么地方见到这么一条长街：一街又大又结实又舒坦的宅子，四周围着绿树和干净的草坪。至少城的这一角没有变；可是变化已经开了头，我相信这就是‘成长’——我认为这就是丹预言的那场不可思议的‘成长’，哈兰。我相信它已经开头了。”

她又一次说得郑重，虽然瞥向他的那一眼里闪着一星半点的得意，这就把他刺了一下。

===Original===

“Well, if this fabulous growth *has* begun,” he said incautiously, “you’re surely not hero-worshipper enough to think it’s going to extend as far as Ornaby Addition, are you?”

She had hoped for this, had led him into it. “Papa’s going to begin building an extension of the Tennessee Avenue car line next month,” she said. “I forced him to admit how far out it would run.”

“Not so far as the Addition?”

“Within an eighth of a mile of it,” said Martha. “That’s what made him so noisy!”

===Chinese===

“好吧，就算这场神话般的‘成长’*当真*开了头，”他一不小心说走了嘴，“你总还不至于崇拜英雄崇拜到那种地步，以为它能一直伸展到奥纳比新区去吧？”

她等的就是这句话，是他自己一步一步被她引进来的。“爸爸下个月就要动工修田纳西大道（Tennessee Avenue）电车线的延长线了，”她说。“那线要伸出去多远，是我逼着他招认的。”

“伸不到新区？”

“离新区不到八分之一英里，”玛莎说。“所以他才吼得那么响！”

## XX / 二十

===Original===
Harlan was astonished, but he took his little defeat well; and Martha in turn encountered a surprise, for he showed a discomfited kind of pleasure. “So Ornaby Addition’s going to get its rapid transit at last,” he said. “That’s not so bad, you know. Why, Dan might come out pretty well on the thing after all!”

“But doesn’t that annoy you, Harlan?” she asked.

“You mean that I *want* to see my brother beaten? That I really haven’t good will toward him?”

“No, indeed I don’t. I mean: Wouldn’t it annoy you to find you’d always been mistaken about him?”

“But I’m not. I grew up in the same house with him, and I ought to know him. If he does happen to do anything with his wild old idea after all, it’ll be by the grace of a series of miracles no one could possibly have foreseen.”

===Chinese===
哈兰（Harlan）吃了一惊，不过这点小小的败仗他吃得颇有风度；玛莎（Martha）反倒遇上一桩意外，因为他露出了一种带着狼狈的欢喜。“这么说，奥纳比新区（Ornaby Addition）到底要通上快捷交通了，”他说。“这还不算坏，你知道。嘿，这么一来，丹（Dan）在这件事上没准儿终究能捞着不少好处呢！”

“可这不叫你恼火吗，哈兰？”她问。

“你是说我*想*看我兄弟输？说我当真对他不怀好意？”

“不，我当真不是那个意思。我是说：要是发觉自己把他看错了一辈子，难道不叫你恼火吗？”

“可我没有看错。我跟他在同一个屋顶底下长大，我该懂得他。就算他那狂想当真歪打正着弄出了什么名堂，那也是全仗着一连串谁也无从预见的奇迹。”

===Original===
“That is to say,” Martha observed, “you’d call him ‘a fool for luck.’ ”

“Let’s put it, I *hope* he is.”

“And you were just telling me *I* didn’t change!” she cried.

“Yes,” he returned placidly;—“it seems we’re neither of us wiser than we used to be. We sit here talking of Dan and his Addition just as we’d have been talking about them if you’d never been away. You really ought to be speaking with a slight foreign accent and unable to put your mind on anything later than the seventeenth century.”

===Chinese===
“这就是说，”玛莎说，“你会管他叫‘傻人有傻福’。”

“这么说吧，我*但愿*他是。”

“你刚才还在说我*没*变呢！”她嚷道。

“是啊，”他平静地答道；“看来我们俩谁也不比从前更聪明。我们坐在这儿谈丹和他的新区，就跟你要是从没出过门、我们坐在这儿谈他们一模一样。你实在该带一点外国口音说话，再什么都想不了十七世纪以后的才对。”

===Original===
She nodded, agreeing. “Yes, it’s queer; and it makes me *feel* a little queer. You go away and stay forever and ever; then you come back home and by the time your trunk’s unpacked you’re ready to wonder if you’ve been away at all;—maybe you’ve just had a long dream. Of course, too, I knew what was going on at home—not through papa!—but some of the girls of our old set here have been faithful about writing, in spite of their every single one of ’em getting married. That makes me feel I belong to the seventeenth century—almost ‘*cinquecento*!’ ”

“I’d prefer the ‘*cinquecento*,’ ” Harlan said, and immediately added: “Not that I care for it myself.”

“What!” she cried, her eyes widening. “You’d even criticize the Renaissance?”

It appeared that he would, and willingly. Offhand he called the Renaissance “a naive movement amusingly overrated and with the single merit that it was better than what had gone before.” Martha was indignant, and they had an argument in which she proved to be no match for him. He had not been abroad since his junior vacation as an undergraduate, but he knew a great deal more about Italy than she did, though she had just come from long residence there. She continued to disagree with him, and presently was surprised by the suspicion that she enjoyed hearing him talk, and in a way, found him congenial in spite of their differences.

===Chinese===
她点点头，表示同意。“是啊，这很怪；而且也叫我*觉得*有点怪。你走了，一走就是天长地久；然后回到家来，等把箱子归置完，你已经疑心自己到底出没出过门了；——也许只不过做了一场长梦。当然，家里的事我也知道——可不是听爸爸说的！——我们那个老圈子里这儿的姑娘们写信倒一直勤，尽管她们一个个全都嫁了人。这叫我觉得自己属于十七世纪——差不多要算‘*cinquecento*’（意大利语：十六世纪）了！”

“我倒宁愿要‘*cinquecento*’，”哈兰说，随即又补了一句：“倒不是我自己喜欢它。”

“什么！”她嚷道，眼睛睁大了。“你连文艺复兴也要批评？”

看来他的确要批评，而且乐于批评。他随口就把文艺复兴说成“一场幼稚的运动，被逗趣儿似的高估了，唯一的功绩是比它前头的东西强些”。玛莎愤愤然，两人争辩了一场，结果证明她远不是他的对手。他自大学三年级那次假期之后再没出过国，可他对意大利懂得的比她多得多，虽说她刚在那儿长住了多年回来。她继续同他争执，不一会儿却吃惊地起了一点疑心：她竟喜欢听他谈话，而且在某种意义上，觉得他同自己气味相投，尽管两人见解相左。

===Original===
“You’re the only person I ever heard of that criticized the Renaissance,” she said, when he got up to go. “You’re all wrong, of course, even if I can’t prove it. You’re too much for me, but that’s only because you’re such an admirable bookworm.”

Then, as he went down the long path to the gate, she observed that his shoulders had acquired a little more habitual stoop in them than she remembered. Otherwise the tall figure might have been that of a thin athlete; and Harlan had a well-shaped head;—she was readily able to comprehend what one of her friends had written her of him: “And Harlan Oliphant seems to be just as sarcastic as he used to be, but he *is* awfully distinguished-looking as he grows older.” Nevertheless, even in this view of his back, Martha found something irritating, something consciously aristocratic, over-fastidious, skeptical, and precise. “That’s just what you are!” she said half-aloud, before she turned to go into the house. “You can be rather fascinating, but you’re really only an admirable bookworm in a nice, clean white collar!”

The admirable bookworm, unconscious that the definition of him had been enlarged, walked down National Avenue, keeping within the continuous shade of the big maple trees and perplexing himself with introspections as he went. He was dry and cold, as he knew, yet far from incapable of ardour, and he had never entirely lost the ardour he felt for Martha; but what surprised him was the renewed liveliness of that ancient pain she evoked within him. He had thought it dead, but evidently it had only fallen into a doze in her absence.

===Chinese===
“你是我平生听说头一个批评文艺复兴的人，”他起身告辞时她说。“你当然全错了，尽管我拿不出凭据。你叫我招架不住，可那不过因为你是个了不起的书蠹。”

然后，当他顺着长长的小径朝大门走去时，她发觉他肩膀里那点惯常的佝偻，比记忆中又添了一分。不然的话，那高高的身影简直像个清瘦的运动家；何况哈兰的头型生得很好；——她顿时了然一位朋友信中写他的话：“哈兰·奥利芬特好像还是同从前一样爱挖苦人，不过他年纪越长，*确乎*越有一副出众的仪表。”话虽如此，纵然只看这个背影，玛莎也觉出一点叫人来火的东西，一种自觉的贵族气派，过分讲究，多疑，而一丝不苟。“你就是这么个人！”她半出声地自语，才转身进屋去。“你固然能相当迷人，可你归根结底不过是个穿着漂亮干净白领子的、了不起的书蠹！”

这位了不起的书蠹，浑然不觉人家给他的定义又添了新义，沿着国民大道走去，一路不离开大枫树连绵的浓荫，边走边让种种内省把自己搅得心事重重。他自知又干又冷，却远非没有热起来的能力；他对玛莎怀有的那点热忱，也从来没有完全丢掉；可叫他吃惊的，是她在他心里唤起的那阵旧痛，竟又活泛起来。他原以为它死了；显然它只是趁她不在，打了个盹儿。

===Original===
Of course he asked himself why he should ache because she had at once resumed with him her old critical attitude, and why, moreover, he should care about her at all. She had almost no coquetry and little more of the quality called “sheer feminine charm”; she was too downright and plain-minded to possess much of either. She was not masculine yet, as her father said with the plaintive irascibility of a man who knows because he has suffered, she was imperious. “A man might as well be dead as bossed to death,” he often complained. And although she was a handsome creature and graceful, Harlan saw a dozen prettier girls at the new Country Club every day that he played golf there. Notwithstanding all this, she had only to let him see her again after years of absence, and at once his heart leaped, then ached, and he could think of nothing but this Martha who thought so little of himself.

He was not the only member of his family who found Martha’s return disturbing; his sister-in-law also had long thoughts connected with the arrival from Italy. That evening before dinner, Dan was whistling in his bathroom, shampooing himself lavishly, when Lena came into his bedroom and addressed him through the open door.

“I suppose you’ve seen her,” she said, and gave utterance to an emotional little titter that quickly stopped his whistling.

===Chinese===
他当然问过自己：她一见面就对他恢复了老一套的挑剔姿态，他何必为之刺痛；再说，他又何苦把她放在心上。她几乎全无卖弄风情的本事，所谓“纯粹的女性魅力”也多不到哪里去；她为人太直、太质朴，这两样在她身上都占不了多少。她也并不男子气，只是专断——这一点她父亲最有体会，说起时总带着过来人那种又哀又恼的口气。“男人叫人管得死死的，还不如死了利索，”他常常这样诉苦。况且，她虽然是个标致优雅的人物，哈兰在新办的乡村俱乐部（Country Club）打高尔夫球的日子，天天都能看见十二个比她更漂亮的姑娘。尽管有这一切，隔了多年不见，她只消再让他见上一面，他的心便立刻跳起来，随即作痛，除了这位把他看得一文不值的玛莎，他什么也想不进去。

发觉玛莎的归来搅得人心绪不宁的，他们家里并非只有他一个；他的嫂嫂心里，也与这位从意大利归来的人牵连着长久的思虑。那天晚饭前，丹正在浴室里吹口哨，酣畅地往头上打着皂沫，莉娜（Lena）走进他的卧室，隔着敞开的门同他说话。

“我看你见过她了，”她说，还发出一声带着情绪的轻笑，立刻把他的口哨止住了。

===Original===
He had heard such semblances of amusement from her often enough to understand their prophetic meaning. “In for it again!” was instantly his thought. “Seen her?” he said. “Who do you mean?”

“Your fair mountain range,” Lena replied, affecting a light mockery. “Of course you didn’t know she’s home again! Innocent old Dannie!”

“I heard she was to get here today, so I suppose she’s here; but I haven’t seen her. What about it?”

“Oh, nothing!” Lena returned, continuing her archness. “Do you suppose she can stand it?”

===Chinese===
她这种佯装有趣的腔调，他听得够多了，深知它们的预言意味。“又要轮到我遭殃了！”他心里立刻想道。“见过她？”他说。“你指谁？”

“你那座秀丽的山脉，”莉娜答道，故作轻轻地挖苦。“你当然不知道她又回来了！天真的老丹尼！”

“我听说她今天到，那想必是到了；可我还没有见着她。这有什么可说的？”

“嗬，没什么！”莉娜答道，那股娇俏劲儿还不肯放。“你想她受得了吗？”

===Original===
“Stand what?”

“Why, the sight of us—of her old sweetheart married to me,” Lena explained. “She’s stayed away till she thought she could bear it, but do you suppose she *will* be able to?”

“Yes, I think she’ll bear it,” he said gruffly and went on with his lathering.

“How about you? Do you think *you’ll* be able to contain yourself when you—”

===Chinese===
“受什么？”

“嗐，看见我们呀——看见她的旧情人娶了我，”莉娜解释道。“她一直躲着不回来，躲到自以为受得住了；可你以为她*果真*受得住吗？”

“是啊，我想她受得住，”他粗声粗气地说，接着打他的皂沫。

“那你呢？等到你——的时候，你以为*你*还按捺得住自己吗？”

===Original===
“I expect so.”

“Why don’t you ask me how she looks?” Lena inquired, still affecting to rally him gaily. “I know you’re dying to. I’ve seen her; I was looking from my window and saw her go out and walk up the street this afternoon. I laughed so!”

“What about?”

“She was such a perfect picture of a big Western woman! Absolutely typical!”

===Chinese===
“我想能。”

“你怎么不问问她如今什么样儿？”莉娜追问，还在装出快快活活逗弄他的样子。“我知道你渴望得要命。我见过她了；下午我从窗口望出去，看见她出了门，顺着大街走上去。我笑坏了！”

“笑什么？”

“她真是一幅活脱脱的西部大女人的画像！典型得十足十！”

===Original===
“You mean like mother, for instance?”

“No; your mother’s a dear thing who’d be lovely anywhere; I never think of her as Western at all,” Lena said. “She isn’t.”

“She is as much as Martha is—or anybody else. She was born here and—”

“Not at all!” Lena interrupted airily. “The real Western woman is like your mountain girl. They *love* to be huge; that’s why they live in the prairie country—so they’ll look even bigger. One reason I laughed was because your friend was just exactly as much the typical Western woman after all this time abroad as she was before she went. She was wearing all kinds of expensive clothes, and I haven’t a doubt she’d got them in Paris, but on her they looked perfectly as Western as if she’d just bought ’em and put ’em on downtown at Kohn & Sons! Do you suppose you’ll be able to control your raptures at all when you meet her again, old innocent Dannie?”

===Chinese===
“你是说像母亲那样，比方说？”

“不；你母亲是个招人疼的可人儿，放在哪儿都好看，我从来不觉得她像个西部人，”莉娜说。“她不是。”

“她跟玛莎一样是西部人——跟谁都一样。她生在这儿，而且——”

“根本不是！”莉娜轻飘飘地打断他。“真正的西部女人就像你那位山乡姑娘。她们*就爱*长得巨大；所以她们才住在大草原上——好显得更庞大些。我笑的一个缘故是：你的朋友在外面待了这么些年，回来还是同她出国以前一模一样的典型西部女人。她穿了一身值钱的衣裳，我毫不怀疑是在巴黎置办的，可穿在她身上，看上去十足是西部的派头，倒像她刚在闹市区的科恩父子商号（Kohn & Sons）买来就地穿上似的！等你再见到她的时候，你以为你还掌控得住自己的狂喜吗，天真的老丹尼？”

===Original===
“See here,” he said, “I wish you’d let me get fixed for dinner. I had a pretty hot day’s work and I’d like to—”

“Of course you would!” Lena said. “You’d like to make yourself beautiful because you’re going to hurry over there to her just as soon as you’ve finished your dinner, aren’t you? That’s what you have been planning, isn’t it?”

“Why, yes; certainly,” he answered. “I’d like to have you go with me. She’s an old friend of mine and all our family; she’s been away a long time, and it wouldn’t look very cordial not to—”

“Why, no; so it wouldn’t!” Lena mocked, but now her mockery was openly acrid. “It wouldn’t look *cordial* and naturally you’d hate to have her think you lacked cordiality—a woman you were so cordial with you wanted your child to grow up to be like her instead of like its mother!—a woman you were so cordial with you had to hold her hand the very day you brought your bride home! It would be *terrible* to have her think—”

===Chinese===
“喂，我说，”他说，“你让我收拾收拾好吃饭吧。我今天这活儿干得够热火的，我想——”

“你当然想！”莉娜说。“你想把自己打扮得漂漂亮亮，因为你一吃完晚饭就要赶去她那儿，对不对？你一直就是这么盘算的，是不是？”

“嗳，是啊；当然，”他答道。“我想让你跟我一块儿去。她是我和我们全家的老朋友；她走了这么多年，不过去招呼一声，显得不够亲热——”

“嗳，可不；就是显得不够亲热嘛！”莉娜挖苦道，不过这会儿她的挖苦已是明晃晃的尖刻了。“是*不亲热*呀，你自然不愿叫她当你缺少热忱——对这么一个女人，你亲热得要自己的孩子长大像她，倒不像他自己的妈！——亲热得把你新娘娶进门的当天还要拉着她的手！要叫她这么想，可就*可怕*了——”

===Original===
But here Dan closed the door, though not so sharply as Lena closed the outer door of his bedroom when she went out of it an instant later.

The subject of Martha’s return was not again mentioned directly during the evening; and after dinner, when Lena with arch significance inquired of her silent husband why he had settled down at the library table to write business letters when there was “so much to do in the neighbourhood,” Dan replied, without looking up, that his letters were important—he’d have to beg to be excused from talking. Lena picked up a book, and retired to the easy-chair and the lamp in the bay window, which had once been Harlan’s favourite reading place; but she did not read. She sat looking steadily at her husband—as he thoroughly and uncomfortably understood, though he kept doggedly at his writing.

After a time his mother and father were heard in the hall, going out; and he knew that they were “going over next door” to bid Martha welcome home. They had not mentioned where they were going, and he understood the significance of their not mentioning it—and so did Lena, as she sat watching him. He wondered why he did not rise and say to her: “There’s an old friend of mine next door; I haven’t seen her for years; I ought to go over and tell her I’m glad she’s home, and I want to! There’s no reason I shouldn’t, and you can make the most of it—I’m going!”

===Chinese===
可是丹在这里关上了门——虽说不像片刻之后莉娜走出他卧室时带上外面那扇门那么脆响。

那天晚上，玛莎归来这个话题再没有正面提起过；饭后，莉娜带着话里有话的娇俏劲儿问她沉默的丈夫：左近明明“有那么多事情可做”，他怎么倒安顿在藏书室的桌子前写起业务信函来了；丹头也不抬，答说他的信要紧——谈话他只得告个便。莉娜拿起一本书，退到凸窗前的安乐椅和灯边去——那里曾是哈兰最爱读书的地方；可她并没有读书。她坐着，目不转睛地盯住丈夫——他对此领会得彻头彻尾，也浑身不自在，只是兀自顽强地写他的信。

过了一会儿，听得他母亲和父亲在门厅里出去；他知道他们是“上隔壁家”欢迎玛莎回来。他们没有提去哪儿，他懂得这不提起的意味——坐在那儿望着他的莉娜也懂得。他不明白自己为什么没有站起来对她说：“隔壁有一位我的老朋友；我多年没见她了；我该过去告诉她，我高兴她回来了，我也想去！没有什么不可以的，你要借题发挥就尽管——我走！”

===Original===
Lena had her own wonderings. She wondered why she was keeping her husband from going. Her thought was that she ought to say: “I don’t think I care for you enough any more to have a right to be jealous. Go to your old friend and tell her you’re glad she’s home again, since you wish to. I’m not so small about it as I’m making you think, and I really don’t care.”

Lena wondered why she did not say this to her husband;—in a manner she wanted to say it, and at the same time she knew that she would say nothing of the kind, but on the contrary, intended to keep him in fear of what she might do if he made any effort to appear “cordial,” as he had said, to Martha. Thus the husband and wife sat—the husband bent over his writing and the wife looking at him, her book in her lap. When she looked away from him it was not to the book that her gaze went, but to the wall across the room, where she saw nothing to please her; and when she had looked at the wall for a time she always looked again at Dan. His own eyes were kept to the writing upon the table, yet he must have been conscious of hers when they were upon him, for a deeper frown came upon his forehead whenever she looked away from the wall and again at him.

After a while Mr. and Mrs. Oliphant were heard returning, and in the library it somehow seemed strange, and like an event out of nature’s order, to hear such brisk and cheerful sounds, when the front door opened, letting in the two voices and their owners simultaneously.

===Chinese===
莉娜也有她自己的纳闷。她纳闷自己为什么拦着丈夫不叫他走。她的心思是，她该这么说：“我想我已经不爱你爱到有权利吃醋的地步了。去找你的老朋友，告诉她你高兴她又回来了，既然你愿意。我没有做出叫你以为的那么小心眼儿，我真的不在乎。”

莉娜纳闷自己为什么不对丈夫说这话；——在某种意义上她想这么说，可同时她知道，自己绝不会说这样的话，反倒打算叫他惧着点儿：他若当真按自己说的，要对玛莎显得“亲热”，她会做出什么来。夫妻俩就这么坐着——丈夫伏案写信，妻子望着他，书摊在她膝头。她的目光离开他时，去的并不是那本书，而是房间对面的墙，那墙上她看不出什么悦目的东西；朝墙看了一阵，她总又要回过头来看丹。他的眼睛一直守着桌上的信纸，可她的目光落在他身上时，他必定有所知觉，因为她每回从墙上收回视线重新落到他身上，他额头上的眉头便蹙得更深。

过了一阵，听得奥利芬特先生和奥利芬特太太（Mr. and Mrs. Oliphant）回来了；当前门打开、把两个声音连同它们的主人一齐放进来的时候，藏书室里不知怎么觉得这轻快欢畅的声响有几分古怪，像一桩乱了自然秩序的事体。

===Original===
“Indeed she *is*!” Mrs. Oliphant was heard to say, while her husband continued a narrative evidently begun outside.

“And I told her so. I said, ‘By George, if an old maid’s a person who just gets lovelier and lovelier, Martha, why, then, maybe you’re—’ ”

But here his voice so abruptly dropped to a mumble no one could have doubted that the suppression was in obedience to a tactful gesture of his wife’s; nor was it difficult to picture this gesture as a movement of Mrs. Oliphant’s hand toward the open door of the library. Immediately afterward the two were heard ascending the stairs; the house again became as quiet as before; Dan went on with his writing, and Lena with her looking and wondering.

===Chinese===
“她*当真是*！”听得奥利芬特太太这么说，她丈夫还在往下讲一篇显然是在门外开了头的叙述。

“我还这么跟她说了呢。我说：‘喏，老天爷，玛莎，要是老姑娘就是一个越长越好看的人，那么，你兴许就——’”

可就在这里，他的声音那么突兀地低了下去，变成一片咕哝，谁也不会怀疑这一收口是服从了太太一个得体的手势；而这手势也不难想见，正是奥利芬特太太的手朝藏书室敞开的门那边一抬。紧接着便听得两人上楼去了；屋子重又像先前一样安静；丹接着写信，莉娜接着看她的人、纳她的闷。

===Original===
Often such a vigil between husband and wife does not end by leaving things as they were before it began. Between the two silent people appear to have taken place communications so imperceptible that neither is definitely aware of them, yet each may be affected by them as if by words spoken. It would seem that there is a danger here; for with couples not well wedded the unspoken words may be too true, or may carry altogether too much revelation. Lena stopped wondering; and then rather slowly it became clear to her that she and her husband no longer cared for each other at all. Long, long she had clung to her belief that she was still in love with him; and now all she had left to her of this was that she could still be jealous of him. “A fine reason for not leaving a man!” she said to herself;—especially a man who really cared about nothing but his business and his boy!

Suddenly she rose from her chair, the book in her lap falling to the floor, where she let it remain; and then she stood still, while Dan glanced up inquiringly from his work and met the strange, examining, hostile look she gave him.

There was a final moment of silence between them before Lena hurried across the room and left him. A minute later Dan rubbed his forehead, wondering again. Upstairs, Lena had not slammed her door.

He had an absentminded impression that something had happened, but as its nature seemed indefinite, and he had now become more interested in his letters than in Martha’s return or Lena’s temper, he bent again to his work and kept at it with zest until after midnight.

===Chinese===
夫妻之间这样的对坐相持，常常并不以一切照旧收场。两个沉默的人之间，仿佛发生过一些细微得无从觉察的沟通，谁也不曾明确意识到它们，可各自都可能受了影响，如同听了出口的话。这里面似乎藏着一种危险；因为对结得不好的夫妇来说，无声的话可能太真实，或者泄露得太多太多。莉娜不再纳闷了；随后，相当缓慢地，一件事在她心里清楚起来：她和丈夫已经全然不再相爱。长久，长久以来，她一直抱紧自己依然爱着他的信念；如今这份爱留给她的全部，是她还能为他吃醋。“好一个不离开男人的理由！”她自言自语道；——尤其是一个当真除了他的生意和儿子、什么都不关心的男人！

她忽然从椅子上站起来，膝上的书掉在地板上，她也听任它躺在那里；然后她站在原地不动，丹从他的活计上狐疑地抬起眼来，正撞上她投来的那种奇异、审视而含着敌意的目光。

两人之间最后沉默了片刻，莉娜便快步穿过房间，离开了他。一分钟之后，丹揉了揉额头，重新纳闷起来。楼上，莉娜没有摔门。

他心不在焉地觉得像是出了什么事，可这事的性质说不分明，而他这会儿对那些信的兴趣已经盖过了玛莎的归来或莉娜的脾气，便又俯到活计上，兴致勃勃地一直做到午夜以后。

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===

Dan did not go next day to bid the returned neighbour welcome home—he thought it better to postpone the call of greeting he should have made at once. He knew he should have made it, if even out of no more than mere neighbourliness; but gradually it became postponed into the indefiniteness that means never, a postponement not without parallel when old friends of husbands return. Meanwhile, Martha was not again mentioned by either Lena or her husband; though this is only to say that she was not orally mentioned between them, but continued to be the subject of their silences. Dan did not dare to go to see her; and his own silence, when he was with his wife, was doggedly protestive, while Lena’s was inscrutable, though she sometimes gave him evidences of a faintly amused contempt. She permitted him to perceive that she despised him, but not to understand whether she despised him because he wanted to see Martha or because he was afraid to do what he wanted.
Once or twice, when he came from his long day’s work, he caught a glimpse of a white figure in the twilight of the Shelbys’ veranda, and waved his hat, and thought a hand waved to him in return; but weeks passed and limp midsummer was almost upon the town before he had speech again with the slighted lady, though the slight was always upon his conscience.
Upon a hot Sunday noon, when his father and mother returned from church, he took them to see the “carpenter shop” he had spent the morning making in the old summerhouse for young Henry—Henry Daniel no longer, at the boy’s own vehement request. The grandparents praised the “carpenter shop” but chided their son for staying away from church to construct it, and their grandson for missing Sunday-school. Dan laughed; he had not been to church in a year; and Henry distorted the cherubic rotundities of his small face into as much ferocity as he could accomplish. “I hate Sunday-school,” he declared; and, as his mother joined them just then, he seized her hand. “I don’t *haf* to go ’lessen I want to. You’ll never get *me* in that ole hole again!”

===Chinese===

第二天，丹没有去欢迎那位归来的邻居回家——他觉得最好把那声本该立刻去道的问候往后推一推。他知道这声问候是应该去道的，哪怕仅仅出于街坊情分；可这一推再推，渐渐推进了那种意味着永不的悬而不决之中——丈夫们的老朋友归家的时候，这样的延宕并不算新鲜。这其间，玛莎再没有被莉娜或她丈夫提起；不过这么说只是指他们嘴上没有提她，她却继续做着他们沉默的内容。丹不敢去看她；他同妻子相对时的沉默，透着一股倔强的抗议味儿，莉娜的沉默则高深莫测，虽然她有时也向他露出些微含着嘲弄的轻蔑。她容他察觉她瞧不起他，却不让他明白，她瞧不起的究竟是他想见玛莎，还是他不敢去做自己想做的事。
有一两回，他结束一整天的长活回家来，暮色苍茫里瞥见谢尔比家游廊上一个白衣的身影，便挥了挥帽子，觉得也有一只手朝他挥动作答；可是几个星期过去，绵软无力的仲夏眼看就要压上这座城，他才重新同那位受了怠慢的女士说上话——尽管这桩怠慢始终压在他的良心上。
一个炎热的星期日中午，父亲母亲从教堂回来，丹带他们去参观他花了一上午在那座旧凉亭里给小亨利搭起来的“木匠铺”——不叫亨利·丹尼尔了，是孩子自己斩钉截铁要求的。祖父母夸了“木匠铺”，却责备儿子为搭它而不去做礼拜，又责备孙子误了主日学校。丹笑了；他已有一年没进过教堂；小亨利把他小脸上一团天使似的圆润，使劲拧出他所能挤出的全部凶相。“我恨主日学校，”他宣布；恰在这时他母亲走了过来，他一把抓住她的手。“我才*用不着*非去不可，除非我自己想去。你们再也*休想*让我进那个老破洞了！”

===Original===

“My gracious!” Dan laughed. “It isn’t as bad as all that. You and I *might* decide to begin goin’ again sometime, Henry.”
